\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}\cdot\Big(P_{t}(w)^{2}-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{4}D_{2,t}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}}{d^{2}}\Big)w^{2}\Big)

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}\cdot\Big(P_{t}(w)^{2}-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d^{2}}\Big)w^{2}\Big)

(By
Lemma

4.2
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}w^{2}(w+r)^{2}(w-r)^{2}-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d^{2}}\Big)w^{2}\Big)

(By factorization of
P_{t}(w)
above)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{3}(w-r)^{2}-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d^{2}}\Big)w^{2}\Big)

(B.178)

where the last inequality is by the discussion above
Equation

B.177
. Now, in order to take the expectation over
w\sim\rho_{t}
(conditioned on
w\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]
), we first compute
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[(w-r)^{2}\mid w\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]]
:

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[(w-r)^{2}\mid w\sim[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]]

\displaystyle\geq\Var_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(w\mid w\sim[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])

(B.179)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\E_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}[(w-w^{\prime})^{2}\mid w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]]

(B.180)

where the last inequality is by
Proposition

B.36
. By
Lemma

B.19
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|\gtrsim\xi\kappa^{3})\geq 1-O(\kappa)

(B.181)

By
Lemma

B.12
, if
w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]
, then

\displaystyle|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{3/2}}|w-w^{\prime}|

(B.182)

Thus, combining the previous two equations gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(|w-w^{\prime}|<\gamma_{2}^{3/2}\xi\kappa^{3}

\displaystyle\mid w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])

(B.183)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|<\xi\kappa^{3}\mid w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])

(B.184)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|<\xi\kappa^{3}\text{ and }w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])}{\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])}

(B.185)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa}{\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])}

(By
Lemma

B.19
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa}{\gamma_{2}}

(By
Lemma

B.15
)

and therefore, by
3.2
, since
\kappa/\gamma_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}\log\log d}
, this is at most
\frac{1}{2}
, since
d
is sufficiently large. Thus,

\displaystyle\E_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}[(w-w^{\prime})^{2}\mid

\displaystyle w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]]

(B.186)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(|w-w^{\prime}|\geq\gamma_{2}^{3/2}\xi\kappa^{3}\mid w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])\cdot\gamma_{2}^{3}\xi^{2}\kappa^{6}

(B.187)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}^{3}\xi^{2}\kappa^{6}

(B.188)

where the last inequality is because
\mathbb{P}_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho_{t}}(|w-w^{\prime}|<\gamma_{2}^{3/2}\xi\kappa^{3}\mid w,w^{\prime}\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])\leq\frac{1}{2}
. By
Equation

B.178
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[|v_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid w\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]]

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{3}\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[(w-r)^{2}\mid w\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}]\Big]

(B.189)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d^{2}}\Big)\Big)

(B.190)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{3}\cdot\gamma_{2}^{3}\xi^{2}\kappa^{6}-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d}\Big)\Big)

(By
Equation

B.180
and
Equation

B.188
)

Since
\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(w\in[\gamma_{2}^{3/4},1-\gamma_{2}^{1/2}])\geq\frac{\gamma_{2}}{4}
by
Lemma

B.15
and the symmetry of
\rho_{t}
, this implies that

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}|v_{t}(w)|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}^{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{6}\xi^{2}\kappa^{6}-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d}\Big)\Big)

(B.191)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}^{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\xi^{2}L(\rho_{t})\gamma_{2}^{6}\xi^{2}\kappa^{6}-O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})L(\rho_{t})}{d}\Big)\Big)

(B.c.
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}
(
3.2
) and
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
implies
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}^{2}\gtrsim\xi^{2}L(\rho_{t})
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\gamma_{2}^{2}\cdot\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{6}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d}\Big)\Big)L(\rho_{t})

(B.192)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{8}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}L(\rho_{t})

(Because
d\geq c_{3}
by
3.2
)

Thus, by
Lemma

B.35
,
\frac{dL(\rho_{t})}{dt}\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{8}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}L(\rho_{t})
. By a similar argument as for the subcase where
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
, since
L(\rho_{0})\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{2}
(by
Lemma

B.7
), if we define

\displaystyle I_{|D_{4}|\geq\xi|D_{2}|}=\{t\leq T_{*,\epsilon}\text{ and }t\text{ in Phase 3, Case 1}\mid|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|\}

(B.193)

then

\displaystyle\int_{I_{|D_{4}|\geq\xi|D_{2}|}}1dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{8}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)

(B.194)

since
L(\rho_{T_{*,\epsilon}})\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon^{2}
. Combining
Equation

B.173
and
Equation

B.194
, we find that the total time spent in Phase 3, Case 1 is at most
O(\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{8}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}))
, as desired.
∎

B.6

Phase 3, Case 2

Next, we analyze Phase 3, Case 2, where
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
. In this case, as we will show, for all
t\geq T_{2}
, we will have
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and
D_{2,t}\leq 0
(
Lemma

B.25
). We will first give an outline of our analysis in this case — our goal is again to show that either
|D_{2,t}|\leq\epsilon
or
|D_{4,t}|\leq\epsilon
within
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}\cdot\textup{poly}(\frac{\log\log d}{\epsilon})
time after the start of Phase 3, Case 2.

Proof Strategy for Phase 3, Case 2

We again show that
|v(w)
is large on average and then use
Lemma

B.35
to show that the loss decreases quickly. However, our argument to show that
|v(w)
is large on average is significantly different from Phase 3, Case 1. We can approximately write

\displaystyle v_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=-(1-w^{2})(P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w))

(B.195)

\displaystyle\approx-(1-w^{2})P_{t}(w)

(B.196)

\displaystyle\approx-(1-w^{2})w(2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{2})

(B.197)

\displaystyle\approx(1-w^{2})w(2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|w^{2})

(B.c.
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\approx-(1-w^{2})w(4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|w^{2}-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|)

(B.198)

Letting
r=\sqrt{\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{2\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}}
be the positive root of
P_{t}(w)
, we obtain

\displaystyle v_{t}(w)

\displaystyle\approx-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|(w-r)(w+r)w(1-w^{2})

(B.199)

This time,
v_{t}(w)
has a negative slope at
r
, meaning that the particles are attracted towards
r
, and we cannot use the same argument as in Phase 3, Case 1.

Instead, the key point of our argument in this case is the following. Recall that
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and
D_{2,t}\leq 0
for all
t\geq T_{2}
— these roughly imply that
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]\geq\gamma_{4}
and
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]\leq\gamma_{2}
. By
3.2
, we have
\gamma_{4}\geq 1.1\gamma_{2}^{2}
— in other words,
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]\geq 1.1(\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}])^{2}
. From this observation, we know that a significant fraction of the particles must be far from
r
— otherwise, if all of the particles are concentrated at
r
, then we would have
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]\approx(\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}])^{2}
, which would give a contradiction.

One complication in showing that the velocity is large is that, a priori, nearly all of the particles could be close to
0
or
1
— their velocities could be small even if they are far from
r
. We make use of the potential function
\Phi
defined in the analysis of Phase 3, Case 1 to avoid this issue. In this case, it turns out that for any two particles
w,w^{\prime}
, their potential difference
|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|
is decreasing (
Lemma

B.20
). We can use this to show that a
1-O(\gamma_{4})
fraction of particles
w
with initializations in
[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
(where
\iota_{\textup{M}}
is defined later) cannot become too close to
0
or
1
in
Lemma

B.21
and
Lemma

B.22
. Intuitively, this is because if
|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|
is bounded by
\log B
for two particles
w,w^{\prime}>0
, and
C
is a sufficiently large constant, then
w\leq(\frac{1}{\log\log d})^{C}
roughly implies that
w^{\prime}\lesssim B(\frac{1}{\log\log d})^{C}
, simply by the definition
\Phi(w)=\log(\frac{w}{\sqrt{1-w^{2}}})
together with some algebraic manipulations. Moreover, if all of the particles
w^{\prime}
in this group are at most
B(\frac{1}{\log\log d})^{C}
, then this implies that
D_{2}
has a very large negative value, and this implies that all particles
w
have a positive velocity (as argued in
Lemma

B.22
), meaning that the particles
w
in this group cannot become smaller than
B(\frac{1}{\log\log d})^{C}
. We can show by a similar argument that the particles having initializations in
[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
cannot become too close to
1
.

As long as the positive root
r
of
P_{t}(w)
is at least
\frac{1}{2}
, this group of particles will have a large velocity as argued in
Lemma

B.26
, since a significant fraction of these particles must be at most
\frac{1}{3}
. We encounter a potential issue when the positive root
r
is at most
\frac{1}{2}
: it is possible that the particles initialized in
[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
get stuck at
r
. We mentioned above that by the constraints
D_{2,t}<0
and
D_{4,t}>0
, there must always be some mass away from
r
. However, this mass may only be a
\gamma_{4}^{5/4}
fraction of the total mass, while the particles initialized at
[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
only have a
1-O(\gamma_{4})
fraction of the total mass. Thus, once
r\leq\frac{1}{2}
, it is possible for the mass located far away from
r
to be disjoint from the set of particles initialized at
[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
. To resolve this, we argue in
Lemma

B.28
that the particles larger than
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})
(which may be close to
1
) will move away from
1
at an exponentially fast rate while
r\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Once these particles, which are larger than
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})
, are sufficiently far away from
1
, they will also contribute a significant amount to the velocity. Thus, we can argue as discussed above that there must be a significant fraction of particles away from
r
due to the constraints
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
(
Lemma

B.29
).

We now begin the formal proof. We first show that as long as
D_{4,t}\geq 0
, the potential difference between any two particles
w,w^{\prime}
is increasing. In particular, this holds during Phase 3, Case 2 (but note that this lemma is no longer applicable during Phase 1 or Phase 2).

Lemma B.20

(
\Phi(w)
and
\Phi(w^{\prime})
Move Closer in Phase 3 Case 2)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and suppose
D_{4,t}\geq 0
for all
t
in some time interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]
. Then, for all
\iota,\iota^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi(w_{t}(\iota))-\Phi(w_{t}(\iota^{\prime}))\Big|

(B.200)

is decreasing as a function of
t
on the interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.20
.

We mostly follow the proof of
Lemma

B.11
. Suppose
\iota,\iota^{\prime}\in[0,1]
such that
\iota\leq\iota^{\prime}
. Then,
w_{t}(\iota^{\prime})\geq w_{t}(\iota)
for all times
t
(by
Proposition

B.33
), and since
\Phi
is an increasing function,
\Phi(w_{t}(\iota^{\prime}))\geq\Phi(w_{t}(\iota))
for all times
t
. Thus, it suffices to show that
\Phi(w_{t}(\iota^{\prime}))-\Phi(w_{t}(\iota))
is decreasing. For convenience, we write
w_{t}^{\prime}=w_{t}(\iota^{\prime})
and
w_{t}=w_{t}(\iota)
. We do a computation similar to that in the proof of
Lemma

B.11
:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(\Phi(w_{t}^{\prime})-\Phi(w_{t}))

\displaystyle=\Big(-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}(w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}-{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)}-{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}(w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}\Big)

(B.201)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\Big(-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w_{t}^{2}-{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)}-{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}w_{t}^{2}\Big)

(By
Lemma

B.10
)

\displaystyle=-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}((w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}-w_{t}^{2})-{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}((w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}-w_{t}^{2})

(B.202)

According to the statement of
Lemma

4.4
,
{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}=4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\cdot\frac{6d+9}{d^{2}-1}
, meaning that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(\Phi(w_{t}^{\prime})-\Phi(w_{t}))

\displaystyle=-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}((w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}-w_{t}^{2})-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\cdot\frac{6d+9}{d^{2}-1}((w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}-w_{t}^{2})

(B.203)

\displaystyle=-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\cdot\Big(1-\frac{6d+9}{d^{2}-1}\Big)\cdot((w_{t}^{\prime})^{2}-w_{t}^{2})

(B.204)

\displaystyle<0

(B.c.
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and
w_{t}^{\prime}>w_{t}>0
, and
d\geq c_{3}
(
3.2
))

Thus, on any interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]
where
D_{4,t}\geq 0
,
\Phi(w_{t}^{\prime})-\Phi(w_{t})
is decreasing, as desired.
∎

In the next lemma, we show an upper bound on
|\Phi(w)-\Phi(w^{\prime})|
, at the beginning of Phase 3, Case 2, for particles
w,w^{\prime}
which are respectively initialized at
\iota,\iota^{\prime}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
. Here
\iota_{\textup{M}}
is defined in the statement/proof of the following lemma.

Lemma B.21
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
. Assume that
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
, i.e. we are in Phase 3, Case 2. Then, there exists
\iota_{\textup{M}}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{R}}]
such that
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
and for all
\iota,\iota^{\prime}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi(w_{T_{2}}(\iota))-\Phi(w_{T_{2}}(\iota^{\prime}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C

(B.205)

for some universal constant
C
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.21
.

Since
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
, we have
\E_{w\sim\rho_{T_{2}}}[{P_{4,d}}(w)]=\gamma_{4}
. Thus, by
Equation

A.4
,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}+6d+8}{d^{2}-1}\E_{w\sim\rho_{T_{2}}}[w^{4}]-\frac{6d+12}{d^{2}-1}\E_{w\sim\rho_{T_{2}}}[w^{2}]+\frac{3}{d^{2}-1}=\gamma_{4}

(B.206)

Since
|w_{t}(\iota)|\leq 1
for all
t\geq 0
and all
\iota\in[-1,1]
, the left-hand side of the above equation is
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]\pm O(\frac{1}{d})
, and rearranging gives

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{4}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.207)

By Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{T_{2}}}(|w|\geq 1/2)\lesssim\gamma_{4}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)\lesssim\gamma_{4}

(B.208)

where the second inequality is because
d\geq c_{3}
by
3.2
. Let
\iota_{0}
be such that
w_{T_{2}}(\iota_{0})=\frac{1}{2}
. Let
\iota_{\textup{M}}=\min(\iota_{0},\iota_{\textup{R}})
. Then,
\iota_{\textup{M}}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{R}}]
, and
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
, because
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{0})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{R}})\lesssim O(\kappa)
by
Proposition

B.5
, meaning that
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\max(\gamma_{4},\kappa)\lesssim\gamma_{4}
(where the last inequality is because
d\geq c_{3}
by
3.2
).

Now, let
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
. Observe that on the interval
[0,T_{2}]
, for all
\iota
,
w_{t}(\iota)
is strictly increasing as a function of
t
, for all
\iota
(see
Lemma

B.8
). Thus, if
{T_{\textup{R}}}
is as defined in
Lemma

B.6
, then for all
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{R}}]
,
|w_{{T_{\textup{R}}}}(\iota)|\gtrsim\xi\kappa^{2}
. In particular, for all
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
, we can write
w_{T_{2}}(\iota)\geq C\xi\kappa^{2}
for some universal constant
C
. Additionally, for
\iota\leq\iota_{\textup{M}}
, we have
w_{T_{2}}(\iota)\leq\frac{1}{2}
by
Proposition

B.33
. Thus, since
\Phi(w)
is increasing in
w
, for
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
we have

\displaystyle\Phi(w_{T_{2}}(\iota))\geq\Phi(C\xi\kappa^{2})=\log\Big(\frac{C\xi\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{1-C^{2}\xi^{2}\kappa^{4}}}\Big)\geq\log(C\xi\kappa^{2})=\log C+\log\xi+2\log\kappa

(B.209)

and

\displaystyle\Phi(w_{T_{2}}(\iota))\leq\Phi(1/2)=\log\Big(\frac{1/2}{\sqrt{3/4}}\Big)=\log(1/\sqrt{3})=-\frac{1}{2}\log 3

(B.210)

Thus, for any
\iota,\iota^{\prime}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi(w_{T_{2}}(\iota))-\Phi(w_{T_{2}}(\iota^{\prime}))\Big|\leq-\frac{1}{2}\log 3-\Big(\log C+\log\xi+2\log\kappa\Big)=2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C^{\prime}

(B.211)

where we have defined the constant
C^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{2}\log 3-\log C
.
∎

In the next lemma, we show that for
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
, during Phase 3, Case 2,
w_{t}(\iota)
can be bounded away from
0
and
1
, provided that the conclusion of
Lemma

B.21
holds at time
t
.

Lemma B.22

(Lower and Upper Bounds on Particles Between
\iota_{\textup{L}}
and
\iota_{\textup{M}}
)
.

Suppose that we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
. Let
s\geq T_{2}
, and suppose that for all
t\in[T_{2},s]
,
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and
D_{2,t}\leq 0
. Further suppose that for all
t\in[T_{2},s]
, the conclusion of
Lemma

B.21
holds, i.e.

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi(w_{s}(\iota))-\Phi(w_{s}(\iota^{\prime}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C

(B.212)

for all
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
, where
\iota_{\textup{M}}
is such that
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
, and for some universal constant
C
. Then, for any
t\in[T_{2},s]
, for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
. Furthermore, for all
t\in[T_{2},s]
, we have
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.22
.

Consider some time
t\in[T_{2},s]
. Suppose that for some
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
,
w_{t}(\iota)\leq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
. Also, suppose
\iota^{\prime}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
such that
\iota^{\prime}>\iota
. Then,

\displaystyle\Phi(w_{t}(\iota^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq\Phi(w_{t}(\iota))+2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C

(By
Equation

B.212
, and b.c.
\Phi
is increasing,
\iota^{\prime}>\iota
and
Proposition

B.33
)

\displaystyle\leq\log\Big(\frac{\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{1-\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}}\Big)+2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C

(B.213)

\displaystyle\leq\log(2\xi^{2}\kappa^{2})+2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C

(B.c.
d\geq c_{3}
,
3.2
)

\displaystyle=\log\xi+C^{\prime}

(For some universal constant
C^{\prime}
)

Thus, because
w\leq\frac{w}{\sqrt{1-w^{2}}}
,

\displaystyle\log w_{t}(\iota^{\prime})\leq\log\Big(\frac{w_{t}(\iota^{\prime})}{\sqrt{1-(w_{t}(\iota^{\prime}))^{2}}}\Big)\leq\log\xi+C^{\prime}

(B.214)

Thus,
w_{t}(\iota^{\prime})\lesssim\xi
and for all
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
, we have shown that
w_{t}(\iota)\lesssim\xi
. In addition,
\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
. We can use this to calculate
D_{2,t}
and
D_{4,t}
:

\displaystyle D_{2,t}

\displaystyle=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[\frac{d}{d-1}w^{2}-\frac{1}{d-1}\Big]-\gamma_{2}

(By definition of
{P_{2,d}}
, (
Equation

A.4
))

\displaystyle\leq\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]-\gamma_{2}

(B.c.
w\leq 1
)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|\leq\iota_{\textup{M}})\E[w_{t}(\iota)^{2}\mid|\iota|\leq\iota_{\textup{M}}]+\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\E[w_{t}(\iota)^{2}\mid|\iota|\geq\iota_{\textup{M}}]-\gamma_{2}

(B.215)

\displaystyle\leq O(\xi^{2})+\gamma_{4}-\gamma_{2}

(B.c.
w_{t}(\iota)\lesssim\xi
for
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
and
\mathbb{P}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\gamma_{2}

(B.c.
\gamma_{4}\leq c_{1}\gamma_{2}^{2}
,
\gamma_{2}\leq c_{2}
(
c_{2}
chosen after
c_{1}
) and
d\geq c_{3}
,
3.2
)

and

\displaystyle D_{4,t}

\displaystyle=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[\frac{d^{2}+6d+8}{d^{2}-1}w^{4}-\frac{6d+12}{d^{2}-1}w^{2}+\frac{3}{d^{2}-1}\Big]-\gamma_{4}

(By definition of
{P_{4,d}}
,
Equation

A.4
)

\displaystyle=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]-\gamma_{4}\pm O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.216)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|\leq\iota_{\textup{M}})\E[w_{t}(\iota)^{4}\mid|\iota|\leq\iota_{\textup{M}}]+\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\E[w_{t}(\iota)^{4}\mid|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}}]-\gamma_{4}\pm O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.217)

\displaystyle\leq O(\xi^{4})+O(\gamma_{4})-\gamma_{4}\pm O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.c.
w_{t}(\iota)\lesssim\xi
for
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
and
\mathbb{P}(\iota>\iota_{\textup{M}})\lesssim\gamma_{4}
)

\displaystyle\leq O(\xi^{4}+\gamma_{4})

(B.218)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{4}

(B.c.
d\geq c_{3}
, by
3.2
)

To complete the proof, we will show that if these bounds on
D_{2,t}
and
D_{4,t}
hold, then for any
w
,
v_{t}(w)\geq 0
. By
Lemma

4.4
, it suffices to show that
P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)\leq 0
for
w\geq 0
, where
P_{t}
and
Q_{t}
are as defined in
Lemma

4.4
. If
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are two appropriately chosen positive universal constants, then by the above upper bounds on
D_{2,t}
and
D_{4,t}
,

\displaystyle P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}+{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)}w+{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}w^{3}

(B.219)

\displaystyle\leq-C_{1}\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}w+C_{2}\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\cdot\gamma_{4}w^{3}+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)w

(B.220)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)w^{3}

(By definition of
{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)},{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}
in
Lemma

4.4
and above bounds on
D_{2,t},D_{4,t}
)

\displaystyle\leq-C_{1}^{\prime}\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}w+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|

(B.c.
\gamma_{4}\lesssim\gamma_{2}^{2}\leq\gamma_{2}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}
by
3.2
, with new constant
C_{1}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}w

(B.c.
d\geq c_{3}
,
3.2
)

In summary, if
w_{t}(\iota)=\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
for some
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
, then
v_{t}(w)>0
for all
w\in[0,1]
. In addition, at time
T_{2}
, we have
w_{t}(\iota)\gtrsim\xi\kappa^{2}
for all
\iota>\iota_{\textup{L}}
by
Lemma

B.8
and
Lemma

B.6
. Thus, for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
,
w_{t}(\iota)
will never go below
\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
for
t\in[T_{2},s]
, as desired.

To finish the proof, we will show the upper bound on
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})
. Suppose
1-w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
. Then,

\displaystyle\Phi(w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}}))

\displaystyle=\log\Big(\frac{w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})}{\sqrt{1-w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})^{2}}}\Big)

(B.221)

\displaystyle\geq\log\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})^{2})

(B.c.
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\geq\frac{1}{2}
)

\displaystyle\geq\log\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-(1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3})^{2})

(B.222)

\displaystyle=\log\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}-\kappa^{8}\xi^{6})

(B.223)

\displaystyle\geq\log\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log(\kappa^{4}\xi^{3})

(B.c.
\kappa,\xi\leq 1
and
\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}-(\kappa^{4}\xi^{3})^{2}\geq 0
)

\displaystyle=\log\frac{1}{2}+2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\frac{3}{2}\log\frac{1}{\xi}

(B.224)

Thus, for any
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
,

\displaystyle\Phi(w_{t}(\iota))

\displaystyle\geq C+\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{1}{\xi}

(B.225)

for some universal constant
C
. In other words, letting
w_{t}:=w_{t}(\iota)
and rearranging using the definition of
\Phi
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{w_{t}}{\sqrt{1-w_{t}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{\xi^{1/2}}

(B.226)

or

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-w_{t}^{2}}\lesssim\xi^{1/2}w_{t}\lesssim\xi^{1/2}

(B.227)

which finally gives
1-w_{t}^{2}\lesssim\xi
, or
w_{t}^{2}\geq 1-O(\xi)
. In other words, for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
, we have
w_{t}(\iota)\geq 1-O(\xi)
, which implies that

\displaystyle D_{2,t}

\displaystyle=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{2,d}}(w)]-\gamma_{2}

(B.228)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]-\gamma_{2}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.229)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\iota\sim\rho_{0}}(|\iota|\geq\iota_{\textup{L}})\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}\mid|\iota|\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}]-\gamma_{2}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.230)

\displaystyle\geq(1-O(\kappa))\cdot(\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}\mid|\iota|\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}])-\gamma_{2}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(By
Proposition

B.5
)

\displaystyle\geq(1-O(\kappa))(1-O(\xi))-\gamma_{2}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.c. if
|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{L}}
, then
w_{t}(\iota)^{2}\geq 1-O(\xi)
)

\displaystyle\geq 1-O(\xi+\kappa)-\gamma_{2}

(B.c.
1/d
is small by
3.2
)

\displaystyle>0

(By
3.2
b.c.
\gamma_{2}
small and
d
large)

and this contradicts the assumption that for all
t\in[T_{2},s]
,
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and
D_{2,t}\leq 0
. Thus, for all
t\in[T_{2},s]
,
1-w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\geq\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
, i.e.
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
.
∎

The purpose of the next two lemmas is to show that if Phase 3, Case 2 occurs, then for all
t\geq T_{2}
, we will have
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
, assuming the conclusion of
Lemma

B.22
holds. We will later put these next two lemmas with the above lemma,
Lemma

B.22
, in order to inductively show that the hypothesis of
Lemma

B.22
holds for
t\geq T_{2}
(note that
Lemma

B.22
assumes that the upper bound on the potential difference holds on an interval
[T_{2},s]
, and more importantly assumes that
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
for all
t\in[T_{2},s]
).

Lemma B.23
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and suppose
D_{4,t}=0
and
D_{2,t}<0
for some
t\geq 0
. Additionally, suppose that the conclusion of
Lemma

B.22
holds at time
t
, i.e. for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
, and
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
. Then,
\frac{d}{ds}D_{4,s}\big|_{s=t}>0
.

Proof.

Recall from
Equation

A.4
that

\displaystyle{P_{4,d}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{2}+6d+8}{d^{2}-1}t^{4}-\frac{6d+12}{d^{2}-1}t^{2}+\frac{3}{d^{2}-1}

(B.231)

meaning that

\displaystyle{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(t)=4t^{3}\pm O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)|t|

(B.232)

Next we compute the derivative of
D_{4,t}
:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}D_{4,t}=\frac{d}{dt}\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}(w)]=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)]

(B.233)

For positive particles
w\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, we have
{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\geq 0
, and for
w\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, it may be the case that
{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\leq 0
. Furthermore, when
D_{4,t}=0
and
D_{2,t}\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle v_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=-(1-w^{2})\Big(2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|\Big)

(By
Lemma

4.4
and because
D_{4,t}=0
)

\displaystyle=(1-w^{2})\cdot\Big(2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w\pm O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|\Big)

(B.c.
D_{2,t}\leq 0
)

\displaystyle=(1-w^{2})\cdot 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w\cdot(1\pm O(1/d))

(B.234)

Finally, to lower bound
\frac{d}{dt}D_{4,t}
, we must show that there is a significant fraction of particles for which
w\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
and
1-w
is large. Because we have
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
, and
\mathbb{P}(|\iota|<\iota_{\textup{L}})\lesssim\kappa
by
Proposition

B.5
, using a union bound we therefore know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}])\geq 1-O(\gamma_{4})-O(\kappa)\geq 1-O(\gamma_{4})

(B.235)

where the second inequality is by
3.2
since
d
is sufficiently large. Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}D_{4,t}

\displaystyle=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)]

(B.236)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\lesssim 1/\sqrt{d})\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\lesssim 1/\sqrt{d}]

(B.237)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{d})\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{d}]

(B.238)

\displaystyle\geq-O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\cdot\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d^{1/2}}\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{d})\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{d}]

(By bounding
|{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)|
when
|w|\lesssim 1/\sqrt{d}
)

\displaystyle\geq-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}])

(B.239)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}]]

(B.240)

\displaystyle\geq-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+(1-O(\gamma_{4}))\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(w)\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}]]

(By
Equation

B.235
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+(\xi^{2}\kappa^{2})^{3}\cdot(1-(1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3})^{2})\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\xi^{2}\kappa^{2})

(B.241)

where the last inequality is by
Equation

B.232
and applying the above bound on
v_{t}(w)
from
Equation

B.234
for
|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}]
, and because
d\geq c_{3}
by
3.2
. Expanding the last line gives

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}D_{4,t}

\displaystyle\gtrsim-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\xi^{6}\kappa^{6}\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}

(B.242)

\displaystyle\gtrsim-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\xi^{11}\kappa^{12}

(B.243)

and the right hand side is strictly positive since
d
is sufficiently large by
3.2
.
∎

Lemma B.24
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and suppose
D_{4,t}>0
and
D_{2,t}=0
for some
t\geq 0
. Additionally, suppose that the conclusion of
Lemma

B.22
holds at time
t
, i.e. for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
, and
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
. Then,
\frac{d}{ds}D_{2,s}\big|_{s=t}<0
.

Proof.

Recall from
Equation

A.4
that

\displaystyle{P_{2,d}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d-1}t^{2}-\frac{1}{d-1}

(B.244)

meaning that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}D_{2,t}=\frac{d}{dt}\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{2,d}}(t)]=\frac{2d}{d-1}\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w\cdot v_{t}(w)]

(B.245)

Suppose
D_{2,t}=0
and
D_{4,t}>0
. Then, the velocity is

\displaystyle v_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=-(1-w^{2})(P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w))

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle=-(1-w^{2})\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}\pm O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|\Big)

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle=-(1-w^{2})\cdot 4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\Big(w^{3}\pm\frac{|w|}{d}\Big)

(B.246)

Thus, for positive particles
w
, if
w\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
,
v_{t}(w)\leq 0
, and for
w\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, the velocity is potentially positive. Using this, we can upper bound
\frac{d}{dt}D_{2,t}
by upper bounding
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w\cdot v_{t}(w)]
:

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w\cdot v_{t}(w)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big(|w|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[w\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big]

(B.247)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big(|w|\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[w\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big]

(B.248)

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{1/2}}\cdot\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d^{3/2}}\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big(|w|\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[w\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big]

(By bounding
w\cdot v_{t}(w)
for
0\leq w\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
)

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big(|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}]\Big)

(B.249)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[w\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}]\Big]

(B.c. for
w\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
,
v_{t}(w)<0
.)

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)+(1-O(\kappa)-O(\gamma_{4}))\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[w\cdot v_{t}(w)\mid|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}]\Big]

(B.250)

where the last inequality is because
\mathbb{P}(|\iota|>\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq O(\gamma_{4})
(by the definition of
\iota_{\textup{M}}
, see statement of
Lemma

B.22
) and
\mathbb{P}(|\iota|<\iota_{\textup{L}})\leq O(\kappa)
by
Proposition

B.5
. Simplifying further, we obtain,

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w\cdot v_{t}(w)]

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d^{2}}\Big)-(1-O(\kappa)-O(\gamma_{4}))(\xi^{2}\kappa^{2})\cdot(1-(1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3})^{2})\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}(\xi^{2}\kappa^{2})^{3}

(By
Equation

B.246
and b.c.
\frac{1}{d}
is small (
3.2
))

\displaystyle\lesssim O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\cdot\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\cdot\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}\xi^{6}\kappa^{6}

(By
3.2
,
1-O(\kappa)-O(\gamma_{4})\gtrsim 1
)

\displaystyle\lesssim O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\xi^{11}\kappa^{12}

(B.251)

and the last expression is strictly negative, since
d\geq c_{3}
by
3.2
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
. This proves the lemma.
∎

Now, we combine
Lemma

B.22
,
Lemma

B.23
and
Lemma

B.24
to inductively show that for all
t\geq T_{2}
, we have
D_{2,t}\leq 0
,
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and most importantly,
w_{t}(\iota)
can be bounded away from
0
and
1
for
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
.

Lemma B.25

(Phase 3 Case 2 Invariants)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
. Assume
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
and
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
, i.e. Phase 3 Case 2 holds. Then, for all
t\geq T_{2}
, the following will hold:

1.

D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0

2.

Let
\iota_{\textup{M}}
be defined as in
Lemma

B.22
. Then,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
for all
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
and
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.25
.

For the purpose of this proof, define

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\{s\geq T_{2}\mid D_{2,s}\leq 0\text{ and }D_{4,s}\geq 0\}

(B.252)

and let
t_{0}=\sup I
, meaning that for
t\in[T_{2},t_{0}]
,
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
. Assume for the sake of contradiction that
t_{0}<\infty
. Then, by the continuity of
D_{2,t}
and
D_{4,t}
as functions of
t
,
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
. Since
t_{0}=\sup I
, either
D_{2,t_{0}}=0
or
D_{4,t_{0}}=0
(and one of these has to be nonzero since otherwise
t_{0}=\infty
, which is a contradiction). By
Lemma

B.21
and
Lemma

B.20
, for all
t\in[T_{2},t_{0}]
, for all
\iota,\iota^{\prime}\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi(w_{t}(\iota))-\Phi(w_{t}(\iota^{\prime}))\Big|\leq 2\log\frac{1}{\kappa}+\log\frac{1}{\xi}+C

(B.253)

where
C
is a universal constant. Thus, applying
Lemma

B.22
with
s=t_{0}
, we find that for all
t\in[T_{2},t_{0}]
, for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
, and furthermore, for all
t\in[T_{2},t_{0}]
,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
.

To finish the proof, there are two cases to address: either
D_{2,t_{0}}<0
and
D_{4,t_{0}}=0
, or
D_{2,t_{0}}=0
and
D_{4,t_{0}}>0
. In the first case, by
Lemma

B.23
,
\frac{d}{dt}D_{4,t}\Big|_{t=t_{0}}>0
, meaning that for a sufficiently small open interval of time
J
containing
t_{0}
,
D_{4,t}
is increasing on this interval. This gives us a contradiction, since it implies that for some
t_{0}^{\prime}>t_{0}
(with
t_{0}^{\prime}-t_{0}
sufficiently small)
D_{4,t}
is nonnegative on
[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]
, and since
D_{2,t_{0}}<0
, by continuity,
D_{2,t}<0
for
t\in[t_{0},t_{0}^{\prime}]
as long as
t_{0}^{\prime}-t_{0}>0
is sufficiently small. Thus,
\sup I>t_{0}
, which is a contradiction. In the second case, we can apply the same argument using
Lemma

B.24
in place of
Lemma

B.23
.

In summary, if
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
, then for all
t\geq T_{2}
,
D_{2,t}\leq 0
and
D_{4,t}\geq 0
. As a consequence of
Lemma

B.20
,
Lemma

B.21
, and
Lemma

B.22
, for all
\iota\in[\iota_{\textup{L}},\iota_{\textup{M}}]
,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
, and furthermore,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{M}})\leq 1-\kappa^{4}\xi^{3}
, for all
t\geq T_{2}
.
∎

We now proceed to bound the running time of Phase 3, Case 2, by dividing it into a few subcases, and showing that the running time that each of these subcases contribute is within our desired bound. The first lemma below bounds the running time spent in the subcase where the positive root
r
of
P_{t}
is larger than
\frac{1}{2}
(and where
D_{4,t}
is large relative to
D_{2,t}
).

Lemma B.26
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and assume that
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
, i.e. we are in Phase 3, Case 2. Then, at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
time
t\geq T_{2}
elapses such that the following hold:

1.

If
r
is the positive root of
P_{t}
(as defined in
Lemma

4.4
) then
r\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

2.

|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|

3.

L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}

Proof of
Lemma

B.26
.

Let
t\geq T_{2}
such that
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
. We can write

\displaystyle P_{t}(w)=2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}=4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w(w-r)(w+r)

(B.254)

where
r
is the positive root of
P_{t}(w)
. We are now going to lower bound the expected velocity while
r\geq\frac{1}{2}
by lower bounding
P_{t}(w)
for a large fraction of
w
.

For all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
, and
t\geq T_{2}
, we have
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
, by
Lemma

B.25
. Thus,
\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(w\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2})\leq 1-O(\kappa)
by
Proposition

B.5
. Additionally, because
D_{2,t}\leq 0
, we have
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]\leq\gamma_{2}+O(1/d)
by
Proposition

B.34
, meaning that by Markov’s inequality,
\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\geq\frac{1}{3})\lesssim\gamma_{2}+1/d\leq\gamma_{2}
, where the last inequality is because
d\geq c_{3}
by
3.2
. By a union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1/3])

\displaystyle\geq 1-O(\gamma_{2}+\kappa)

(B.255)

For particles
w
such that
|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1/3]
, we can lower bound the velocity if
r\geq\frac{1}{2}
:

\displaystyle|v_{t}(w)|

\displaystyle\gtrsim(1-w^{2})|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

(B.c.
w\leq 1/3
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim|P_{t}(w)|-|Q_{t}(w)|

(B.256)

\displaystyle\gtrsim|2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}|-|{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)}w+{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}w^{3}|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim|2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}|-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}|w||w-r||w+r|-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)

(By definition of
r
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\cdot\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{6}\cdot\frac{1}{2}-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)

(B.c.
w\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1/3]
and
r\geq 1/2
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}

(B.257)

where the last inequality is because
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
d
is sufficiently large by
3.2
. Thus,

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}|v_{t}(w)|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1/3])\cdot\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[|v_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1/3]]

(B.258)

\displaystyle\gtrsim(1-O(\gamma_{2}+\kappa))\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(By
Equation

B.255
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(By
3.2
since
\gamma_{2},d
sufficiently small, large resp.)

and

\displaystyle\frac{dL(\rho_{t})}{dt}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}D_{4,t}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(By
Lemma

B.35
)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}(D_{4,t}^{2}+\xi^{2}D_{2,t}^{2})\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(B.c.
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}(D_{4,t}^{2}+D_{2,t}^{2})\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}

(B.259)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}L(\rho_{t})

(B.c.
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\asymp\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
by
3.2
)

Since
L(\rho_{0})\asymp\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{2}
by
Lemma

B.7
and
3.2
, the amount of time spent in the case where
r(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{4,t}|
and
L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
after
t\geq T_{2}
is at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
by Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
).
∎

Next, we deal with the subcase where
D_{4,t}
is small relative to
D_{2,t}
.

Lemma B.27
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
, i.e. we are in Phase 3, Case 2. Then, at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
time
t\geq T_{2}
elapses such that the following hold:

1.

|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|

2.

L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}

Proof of
Lemma

B.27
.

Suppose
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
at some time
t\geq T_{2}
. Then, we can lower bound the velocity for a large fraction of
w
’s, as follows. First, we provide a bound on
P_{t}(w)
(defined in
Lemma

4.4
):

\displaystyle P_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle=-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|w^{3}

(B.c.
D_{2,t}<0
and
D_{4,t}>0
by
Lemma

B.25
)

\displaystyle=-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w+O(\xi\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w^{3})

(B.c.
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
)

\displaystyle=-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w+O(\xi\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w^{3})

(B.c.
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
by
3.2
)

\displaystyle=-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w\cdot(1-O(\xi))

(B.260)

Next, we provide a bound on
Q_{t}(w)
:

\displaystyle|Q_{t}(w)|

\displaystyle=|{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)}w+{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}w|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|

(B.c.
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}
)

\displaystyle=O(\xi\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|)|w|

(B.c.
1/d\leq\frac{1}{\log\log d}=\xi
by
3.2
)

Thus,

\displaystyle|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

\displaystyle\geq 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w\cdot(1-O(\xi))

(B.261)

By
Lemma

B.25
, we have
D_{2,t}\leq 0
, meaning that by
Proposition

B.34
,

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{2}+O(1/d)

(B.262)

Thus, by Markov’s inequality and
3.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\geq 1/2)\lesssim\gamma_{2}

(B.263)

Furthermore, by
Lemma

B.25
, for all
\iota\geq\iota_{\textup{L}}
,
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
for all times
t\geq T_{2}
, meaning that
w_{t}\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
with probability at least
1-O(\kappa)
under
\rho_{t}
, by
Proposition

B.5
. Thus, by a union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\in[\xi^{2}\kappa^{2},1/2])

\displaystyle\geq 1-O(\gamma_{2})-O(\kappa)\geq\frac{1}{2}

(B.264)

where the last inequality is by
3.2
since
\gamma_{2}
and
d
are sufficiently small and large respectively. Thus the average velocity is at least

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}|v_{t}(w)|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2)\cdot\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[|v_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2]

(B.265)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{2}\cdot\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[|v_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2]

(B.266)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[|v_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2]

(B.267)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[(1-w^{2})^{2}|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2]

(B.268)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|^{2}\mid\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2]

(B.c.
|w|\leq 1/2
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{4}|D_{2,t}|^{2}w^{2}\mid\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1/2]

(By
Equation

B.261
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{4}|D_{2,t}|^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(B.269)

Thus, at any time
t
where
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{dL(\rho_{t})}{dt}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{4}|D_{2,t}|^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(B.270)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|^{2})\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}

(B.c.
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}
by
3.2
)

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}L(\rho_{t})

(B.271)

Since the initial loss is
L(\rho_{0})\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{2}
by
Lemma

B.7
, by an argument similar to that used in
Lemma

B.26
, using Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
), we find that

\displaystyle\int_{T_{2}}^{\infty}1(|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|)1(L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2})dt\lesssim\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)

(B.272)

i.e. the time spent in the subcase in the statement of this lemma is at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
, as desired.
∎

We have already considered the case where
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
r\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Next, we must deal with the case where
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
, but where
r\leq\frac{1}{2}
. We further split this case into two subcases. In the next lemma, we deal with the subcase where
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\geq 1-\xi
, meaning a non-negligible portion of the particles are close to
1
.

Lemma B.28
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
, i.e. we are in Phase 3, Case 2. Then, the amount of time
t\geq T_{2}
that elapses while the following conditions simultaneously hold:

1.

|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|

2.

r\leq\frac{1}{2}
where
r
is the positive root of
P_{t}

3.

w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\geq 1-\xi

4.

L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}

is at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\epsilon\xi^{7}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.28
.

If
w(\iota_{\textup{R}})\geq 1-\xi
at any time, then the subsequent times when it could possibly be moving to towards
1
are:

1.

During phase 2 — note that before phase 2,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\leq\frac{1}{\log d}
, so it is not possible for
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})
to be larger than
1-\xi
since, by
Lemma

B.8
,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})
is increasing during Phase 1.

2.

The times
t\geq T_{2}
when
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|

3.

The times
t\geq T_{2}
when
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
r\geq\frac{1}{2}
, where
r
is the positive root of
P_{t}(w)

The only case not mentioned above consists of the times
t\geq T_{2}
when
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and the positive root
r
of
P_{t}(w)
is less than
\frac{1}{2}
. In this case,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})
will be moving away from
1
. For this reason, we also do not need to consider the case discussed in
Lemma

B.29
.

Thus, we will use upper bounds on the velocity and time during the first 3 cases listed above, and use a lower bound on the velocity in the last. First note that the velocity can always be upper bounded as follows:

\displaystyle v_{t}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-w)(1+w)\cdot|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-w)(1+w)\cdot\Big|2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|w^{3}+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|\Big|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-w)\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|)

(B.c.
|w|\leq 1
)

Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(1-w)\gtrsim-(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|)(1-w)\gtrsim-(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(1-w)

(B.273)

During Phase 2.

By
Lemma

4.9
, the running time during Phase 2 is
T_{2}-T_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\xi^{6}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
. At the beginning of Phase 2,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\leq\frac{1}{\log d}
, by the definition of Phase 1. Thus, by Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
), at time
T_{2}
,

\displaystyle 1-w_{T_{2}}(\iota_{\textup{R}})

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\cdot\exp\Big(-(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\xi^{6}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

(By
G.13
,
Equation

B.273
and bound on
T_{2}-T_{1}
)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{\xi^{6}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

(B.274)

Times
t\geq T_{2}
when
|D_{4,t}|\leq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
.

By
Lemma

B.27
, this case contributes at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
time to the overall running time. Thus, by Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
), the additional factor that this case contributes to
1-w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})
is larger than or equal to

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Big(-\frac{C}{\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

(B.275)

for some universal constant
C
.

Times
t\geq T_{2}
when
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
,
r(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
.

By
Lemma

B.26
, this case contributes at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
to the overall running time. Thus, by Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
), the additional factor that this case contributes to
1-w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})
is larger than or equal to

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Big(-\frac{C}{\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

(B.276)

for some universal constant
C
.

Times
t\geq T_{2}
when
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
,
r(t)\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
{w_{\textup{R}}}\geq 1-\xi
.

In this case, we obtain a lower bound on
\frac{d}{dt}(1-{w_{\textup{R}}})
, where we have written
{w_{\textup{R}}}=w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})
for convenience. First let us obtain a lower bound on
P_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})
:

\displaystyle P_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}{w_{\textup{R}}}+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}{w_{\textup{R}}}^{3}

(B.277)

\displaystyle\gtrsim 4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}{w_{\textup{R}}}({w_{\textup{R}}}-r)({w_{\textup{R}}}+r)

(B.c.
r
is the positive root of
P_{t}
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}(1-\xi-r)

(B.c.
D_{4,t}\geq 0
and
{w_{\textup{R}}}\geq 1-\xi
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}

(B.c.
r\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\xi\leq\frac{1}{\log\log d}
)

Next let us obtain an upper bound on
Q_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})
:

\displaystyle|Q_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})|

\displaystyle\lesssim|{\lambda_{d}}^{(1)}|+|{\lambda_{d}}^{(3)}|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{\xi d}

(B.c.
|D_{2,t}|\leq|D_{4,t}|/\xi
and
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
by
3.2
)

Thus, because
\frac{1}{\xi d}\asymp\frac{\log\log d}{d}
,

\displaystyle P_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})+Q_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}

(B.278)

and when
{w_{\textup{R}}}\geq 1-\xi
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(1-{w_{\textup{R}}})

\displaystyle\gtrsim-v_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})

(B.279)

\displaystyle\gtrsim(1-{w_{\textup{R}}})(1+{w_{\textup{R}}})(P_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}})+Q_{t}({w_{\textup{R}}}))

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim(1-{w_{\textup{R}}})\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}

(By
Equation

B.278
and b.c.
{w_{\textup{R}}}\geq 1-\xi
)

Furthermore, since
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
, this means that
|D_{4,t}|\gtrsim\xi\epsilon
, since otherwise,
|D_{2,t}|\lesssim\epsilon
and
|D_{4,t}|\lesssim\xi\epsilon
, and the loss would be less than
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
. Thus, if time
{T_{\textup{esc}}}
is spent in this case, then by Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
), this case contributes a factor of at least

\displaystyle\exp({T_{\textup{esc}}}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t})

\displaystyle\geq\exp({T_{\textup{esc}}}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\xi\epsilon)

(B.280)

to
1-{w_{\textup{R}}}
.

Summary of 4 Cases.

In summary, by
Equation

B.274
,
Equation

B.275
,
Equation

B.276
and
Equation

B.280
,

\displaystyle 1-{w_{\textup{R}}}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{\xi^{6}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)\exp\Big(-\frac{C}{\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

(B.281)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\exp\Big(-\frac{C}{\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)\exp({T_{\textup{esc}}}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\xi\epsilon)

(B.282)

where
{T_{\textup{esc}}}
is the amount of time spent so far in the case where
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
,
r(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
{w_{\textup{R}}}\geq 1-\xi
. This is in fact a lower bound on
1-{w_{\textup{R}}}
when
L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
, since at all other times when
L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
, we have that
1-{w_{\textup{R}}}
is increasing (i.e.
{w_{\textup{R}}}
is moving away from
1
). In particular, if

\displaystyle{T_{\textup{esc}}}\gtrsim\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\xi\epsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{\xi^{6}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+\frac{C}{\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+\frac{C}{\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)\gtrsim\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\epsilon\xi^{7}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)

(B.283)

then
1-{w_{\textup{R}}}
will be at least
\xi
.
∎

Finally, the following lemma deals with the last case, where the positive root
r
of
P_{t}
is at most
\frac{1}{2}
, and additionally,
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\leq 1-\xi
.

Lemma B.29
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
. Assume
D_{4,T_{2}}=0
and
D_{2,T_{2}}<0
, i.e. Phase 3 Case 2 holds. Then, the amount of time
t\geq T_{2}
that elapses while the following conditions simultaneously hold:

1.

|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|

2.

r\leq\frac{1}{2}

3.

w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\leq 1-\xi

4.

L(\rho_{t})\geq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}

is at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{11/2}\xi^{8}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.29
.

For convenience and ease of presentation, in this proof we define
\tau:=\sqrt{\gamma_{2}}
, and
\beta\geq 1.1
such that
\gamma_{4}=\beta\tau^{4}
. (Note that by
3.2
,
\beta
is at most a universal constant.) We first show that there is always a large fraction of particles which are far away from the positive root
r
of
P_{t}(w)
. We consider two cases: (i)
r\leq\tau^{3/2}
and (ii)
r\geq\tau^{3/2}
.

Case 1:
r\leq\tau^{3/2}
.

Assume for the sake of contradiction that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\geq r+\tau^{3/2})

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{4}

(B.284)

Then,

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\geq r+\tau^{3/2})+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\leq r+\tau^{3/2})\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}\mid|w|\leq r+\tau^{3/2}]

(B.285)

\displaystyle=\tau^{4}+(r+\tau^{3/2})^{4}

(B.286)

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{4}+(2\tau^{3/2})^{4}

(By assumption that
r\leq\tau^{3/2}
)

\displaystyle=\tau^{4}+16\tau^{6}

(B.287)

Thus,

\displaystyle D_{4,t}

\displaystyle=\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{4,d}}(w)]-\beta\tau^{4}

(B.288)

\displaystyle\leq\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)-\beta\tau^{4}

(By
Equation

A.4
)

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{4}+16\tau^{6}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)-\beta\tau^{4}

(B.289)

\displaystyle=16\tau^{6}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)-(\beta-1)\tau^{4}

(B.290)

\displaystyle\leq 17\tau^{6}-(\beta-1)\tau^{4}

(B.c.
d
is sufficiently large by
3.2
)

\displaystyle<0

(B.c.
(\beta-1)>17\tau^{2}
by
3.2
)

which contradicts the assumption that we are in Phase 3, Case 2 since
D_{4,t}>0
by
Lemma

B.25
. Thus, if
r\leq\tau^{3/2}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\geq r+\tau^{3/2})\geq\tau^{4}

(B.291)

Case 2:
r\geq\tau^{3/2}
.

Suppose that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(r-\tau^{3/2}\leq|w|\leq r+\tau^{3/2})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\tau^{5}

(B.292)

Then,

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(r-\tau^{3/2}\leq|w|\leq r+\tau^{3/2})\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}\mid|w|\in[r-\tau^{3/2},r+\tau^{3/2}]]

(B.293)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\tau^{5})\cdot(r-\tau^{3/2})^{2}

(B.294)

Since
D_{2,t}<0
by
Lemma

B.25
, this implies that
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]\leq\tau^{2}+O(1/d)
by
Proposition

B.34
. Thus,

\displaystyle(1-\tau^{5})(r-\tau^{3/2})^{2}\leq\tau^{2}+O(1/d)

(B.295)

and taking square roots gives

\displaystyle(1-\tau^{5})^{1/2}(r-\tau^{3/2})\leq\tau+O(1/\sqrt{d})

(B.296)

Thus,

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{3/2}+\frac{1}{(1-\tau^{5})^{1/2}}\cdot\Big(\tau+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)\Big)

(B.297)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\in[r-\tau^{3/2},r+\tau^{3/2}])\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}\mid|w|\in[r-\tau^{3/2},r+\tau^{3/2}]]

(B.298)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\not\in[r-\tau^{3/2},r+\tau^{3/2}])

(B.299)

\displaystyle\leq(r+\tau^{3/2})^{4}+\tau^{5}

(By the assumption in
Equation

B.292
)

and since
D_{4,t}\geq 0
by
Lemma

B.25
,
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{4}]\geq\beta\tau^{4}-O(1/d)
by
Equation

A.4
and because
w\sim\rho_{t}
are bounded by
1
in absolute value. Thus, by the above equation,

\displaystyle(r+\tau^{3/2})^{4}+\tau^{5}

\displaystyle\geq\beta\tau^{4}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.300)

Rearranging gives

\displaystyle(r+\tau^{3/2})^{4}+\tau^{5}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)\geq\beta\tau^{4}

(B.301)

and taking fourth roots gives

\displaystyle r+\tau^{3/2}+\tau^{5/4}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{1/4}}\Big)\geq\beta^{1/4}\tau

(B.302)

(by the inequality
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
). Therefore,

\displaystyle r\geq\beta^{1/4}\tau-\tau^{3/2}-\tau^{5/4}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{1/4}}\Big)

(B.303)

Combining
Equation

B.297
and
Equation

B.303
gives

\displaystyle\beta^{1/4}\tau-\tau^{3/2}-\tau^{5/4}-O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{1/4}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{3/2}+\frac{1}{(1-\tau^{5})^{1/2}}\cdot\Big(\tau+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)\Big)

(B.304)

Rearranging this equation gives

\displaystyle\Big(\beta^{1/4}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tau^{5}}}\Big)\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\tau^{3/2}+\tau^{5/4}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{1/4}}\Big)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tau^{5}}}\cdot O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)

(B.305)

\displaystyle\leq 3\tau^{5/4}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{1/4}}\Big)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tau^{5}}}\cdot O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)

(B.c.
\tau\leq 1
, we have
\tau^{3/2}\leq\tau^{5/4}
)

\displaystyle\leq 4\tau^{5/4}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tau^{5}}}\cdot O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)

(B.c.
d
is sufficiently large by
3.2
)

\displaystyle\leq 4\tau^{5/4}+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big)

(B.c.
\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}
by
3.2
)

\displaystyle\leq 5\tau^{5/4}

(B.c.
d
is sufficiently large by
3.2
)

Further rearranging gives

\displaystyle\beta^{1/4}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tau^{5}}}

\displaystyle\leq 5\tau^{1/4}

(B.306)

This contradicts
3.2
— since
\beta\geq 1.1
, the left-hand side is larger than an absolute constant, while the right-hand side can be made sufficiently small (since
\gamma_{2}\leq c_{2}
and
c_{2}
is chosen to be sufficiently small). Thus, in the case that
r\geq\tau^{3/2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\not\in[r-\tau^{3/2},r+\tau^{3/2}])\geq\tau^{5}

(B.307)

In summary, whether
r\geq\tau^{3/2}
or
r\leq\tau^{3/2}
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(|w|\not\in[r-\tau^{3/2},r+\tau^{3/2}])\geq\tau^{5}

(B.308)

Further assume that
w_{t}(\iota_{\textup{R}})\leq 1-\xi
, and note that by
Lemma

B.25
, for all
\iota>\iota_{\textup{L}}
, we have
w_{t}(\iota)\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
. Thus, by
Proposition

B.5
, with probability
1-O(\kappa)
under
\rho_{t}
, we have
\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1-\xi
. By a union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(||w|-r|\geq\tau^{3/2}\text{ and }\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1-\xi)\geq\tau^{5}-O(\kappa)\geq\frac{\tau^{5}}{2}

(B.309)

Suppose
w
satisfies
||w|-r|\geq\tau^{3/2}
and
\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1-\xi
, i.e. the conditions mentioned in
Equation

B.309
, and write

\displaystyle P_{t}(w)=2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2,t}w+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w^{3}=4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w(w-r)(w+r)

(B.310)

Then,

\displaystyle|v_{t}(w)|

\displaystyle\gtrsim|1-w^{2}|\cdot|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim|1+w|\cdot|1-w|\cdot|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

(B.311)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\xi|P_{t}(w)+Q_{t}(w)|

(B.c.
|w|\leq 1-\xi
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\xi\cdot\Big|4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4,t}w(w-r)(w+r)-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|w|\Big|

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\xi\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\cdot\tau^{3/2}\cdot\tau^{3/2}-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)\Big)

(By triangle inequality and b.c.
|w|\geq\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}
and
||w|-r|\geq\tau^{3/2}
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\xi\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|\tau^{3}\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}-O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{\xi d}\Big)\Big)

(B.c.
|D_{2,t}|\leq|D_{4,t}|/\xi
and
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
by
3.2
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\xi\cdot 4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|\tau^{3}\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}

(B.c.
1/(\xi d)=\frac{\log\log d}{d}
and
d
is sufficiently large (
3.2
))

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\tau^{3}\xi^{3}\kappa^{2}|D_{4,t}|

(B.312)

and the average velocity is at least

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}|v_{t}(w)|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\mathbb{P}_{w\sim\rho_{t}}(||w|-r|\geq\tau^{3/2}\text{ and }\xi^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq|w|\leq 1-\xi)\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}\tau^{6}\xi^{6}\kappa^{4}|D_{4,t}|^{2}

(By
Equation

B.312
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\tau^{5}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}\tau^{6}\xi^{6}\kappa^{4}|D_{4,t}|^{2}

(By
Equation

B.309
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{4}\tau^{11}\xi^{6}\kappa^{4}|D_{4,t}|^{2}

(B.313)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\tau^{11}\xi^{6}\kappa^{4}(\xi^{2}L(\rho_{t}))

(B.c.
|D_{4,t}|\geq\xi|D_{2,t}|
and
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}
by
3.2
)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\tau^{11}\xi^{8}\kappa^{4}L(\rho_{t})

(B.314)

Thus, by
Lemma

B.35
,

\displaystyle\frac{dL(\rho_{t})}{dt}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\tau^{11}\xi^{8}\kappa^{4}L(\rho_{t})

(B.315)

Since the initial loss is
L(\rho_{0})\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}^{2}
by
Lemma

B.7
, we can calculate using Gronwall’s inequality (
G.13
) that the time spent when the conditions in the statement of this lemma simultaneously hold is at most
\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\tau^{11}\xi^{8}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
, as desired.
∎

To conclude, we show
Lemma

4.11
which gives a bound on the running time during Phase 3, Case 2.

Proof of
Lemma

4.11
.

By combining
Lemma

B.26
,
Lemma

B.27
,
Lemma

B.28
, and
Lemma

B.29
, we find that the overall running time during Phase 3 Case 2 is at most

\displaystyle O\Big(\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\xi^{4}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\epsilon\xi^{7}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{11/2}\xi^{8}\kappa^{4}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)

(B.316)

and this can be upper bounded by
O\Big(\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{11/2}\xi^{8}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)
, where we have used the fact that
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\asymp\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
(see
3.2
).
∎

B.7

Summary of Phase 3

The following lemma provides a bound on the running time after
T_{2}
that is required for
L(\rho_{t})\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}
.

Lemma B.30

(Phase 3)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
. Then,
T_{*,\epsilon}-T_{2}\lesssim\frac{(\log\log d)^{20}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{8}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.30
.

By combining
Lemma

4.10
and
Lemma

4.11
, we find that the overall running time during Phase 3 is at most

\displaystyle\max\Big(O\Big(\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}^{8}\xi^{4}\kappa^{6}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big),O\Big(\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{11/2}\xi^{8}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)\Big)

(B.317)

and this is at most
O\Big(\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{8}\xi^{8}\kappa^{12}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big)
, where we have used the fact that
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\asymp\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
(see
3.2
)
∎

B.8

Proof of Main Theorem for Population Case

Finally, we prove
Theorem

3.3
:

Proof of
Theorem

3.3
.

We combine
Lemma

4.8
,
Lemma

4.9
and
Lemma

B.30
to find that the total runtime is at most

\displaystyle T_{*,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}}\log d+\frac{(\log\log d)^{18}}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)+\frac{(\log\log d)^{20}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{8}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)

(B.318)

and we can further simplify the above bound to obtain:

\displaystyle T_{*,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\gamma_{2}}\log d+\frac{(\log\log d)^{20}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{8}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)

(B.319)

where we have used the fact that
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\asymp\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}
by
3.2
.
∎

B.9

Additional Lemmas

Lemma B.31
.

Suppose
3.2
holds. There exists some
r
, with
r\gtrsim\frac{1}{\log\log d}
and
r\leq 1
, such that if
\rho
is the singleton distribution under which
r
has probability
1
, then the velocity at
r
is
0.

Proof.

Let
\rho
be such that
r
has probability
1
under
\rho
, for some
r\in(0,1)
. Applying the definitions of
D_{2}
and
D_{4}
to
\rho
, we have
D_{2}={P_{2,d}}(r)-\gamma_{2}
and
D_{4}={P_{4,d}}(r)-\gamma_{4}
. By
Lemma

B.3
, the velocity of
r
is

\displaystyle v(r)

\displaystyle=-(1-r^{2})(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}D_{2}{P_{2,d}}^{\prime}(r)+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}D_{4}{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(r))

(B.320)

The second factor is equal to

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}({P_{2,d}}(r)-\gamma_{2}){P_{2,d}}^{\prime}(r)+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}({P_{4,d}}(r)-\gamma_{4}){P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(r)

(B.321)

and we denote this polynomial of
r
by
F(r)
. Note that
{P_{2,d}}(1)={P_{4,d}}(1)=1
(see Equation (2.20) of
Atkinson and Han 2012
). Thus,
F(1)>0
by
3.2
, since
{P_{2,d}}^{\prime}(1)>0
and
{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(1)>0
by
Equation

A.4
. On the other hand, suppose
r\asymp\frac{1}{\log\log d}
. Then,

\displaystyle|{P_{2,d}}(r)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{d}{d-1}r^{2}-\frac{1}{d-1}\Big|

(By
Equation

A.4
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\log\log d)^{2}}

(B.322)

Similarly,
|{P_{4,d}}(r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{(\log\log d)^{4}}
. Thus,
{P_{2,d}}(r)-\gamma_{2}<0
and
{P_{4,d}}(r)-\gamma_{4}<0
since
d
can be chosen to be sufficiently large according to
3.2
. However, by
Equation

A.4
, one can show that
{P_{2,d}}^{\prime}(r)\gtrsim\frac{1}{\log\log d}
and
{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(r)\gtrsim\frac{1}{(\log\log d)^{3}}
as long as
d
is sufficiently large. Thus,
F(r)<0
for
r\asymp\frac{1}{\log\log d}
. In summary,
F
has a root between
\frac{C}{\log\log d}
and
1
for some universal constant
C
, and for this value of
r
, the velocity of
r
is
0
.
∎

Lemma B.32
.

In the setting of
Theorem

3.3
,
T_{*,\epsilon}-T_{1}\lesssim\frac{(\log\log d)^{20}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{8}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
. Thus,
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}(T_{*,\epsilon}-T_{1})\lesssim\frac{(\log\log d)^{20}}{\epsilon\gamma_{2}^{7}}\log\Big(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\epsilon}\Big)
.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemma

4.9
and
Lemma

B.30
.
∎

B.10

Helper Lemmas

In this subsection, we give some convenient facts that we use in the following subsections.

Proposition B.33
.

If
0\leq\iota_{1}<\iota_{2}
, then for all
t\geq 0
,
w_{t}(\iota_{1})<w_{t}(\iota_{2})
.

Proof of
Proposition

B.33
.

Assume this is not the case, and let
t
be the infimum of all times
s
such that
w_{s}(\iota_{1})\geq w_{s}(\iota_{2})
. Then,
w_{t}(\iota_{1})=w_{t}(\iota_{2})
, meaning that for all
t^{\prime}\geq t
,
w_{t^{\prime}}(\iota_{1})=w_{t^{\prime}}(\iota_{2})
. For convenience, let
w_{0}=w_{t}(\iota_{1})=w_{t}(\iota_{2})
. Note that
t>0
since
\iota_{1}\neq\iota_{2}
. However, this implies that for
t^{\prime}\in(0,t)
,
w_{t^{\prime}}(\iota_{1})<w_{t^{\prime}}(\iota_{2})
, meaning that for any
\delta>0
,
w_{s}(\iota_{1})
and
w_{s}(\iota_{2})
are non-identical solutions to the initial value problem on the time interval
(t-\delta,t+\delta)
given by the conditions
f(t)=w_{0}
and
\frac{df}{dt}=v_{t}(f(t))
. This contradicts the Picard-Lindelof theorem, and thus there cannot exist a time
t
such that
w_{t}(\iota_{1})\geq w_{t}(\iota_{2})
.
∎

Proposition B.34
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
, and let
t\geq 0
such that
D_{2,t}<0
. Then,
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]\leq\gamma_{2}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)
.

Proof of
Proposition

B.33
.

Suppose
D_{2,t}\leq 0
. Then,
\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[{P_{2,d}}(w)]\leq\gamma_{2}
, which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d-1}\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]-\frac{1}{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{2}

(B.323)

and rearranging gives

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\rho_{t}}[w^{2}]\leq\frac{d-1}{d}\gamma_{2}+\frac{1}{d}\leq\gamma_{2}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)

(B.324)

as desired.
∎

Lemma B.35

(Decrease in Loss and Average Velocity)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.3
. Then,
\frac{dL}{dt}=-\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}[\|{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})\|_{2}^{2}]
.

We note that a similar result was shown in Lemma E.11 of
Wei et al. 2019a
, but for gradient flow rather than projected gradient flow.

Proof of
Lemma

B.35
.

First, we can calculate

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}L(\rho_{t})=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))^{2}]=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))\frac{d}{dt}f_{\rho_{t}}(x)\Big]\,.

(B.325)

Furthermore, we can expand
\frac{d}{dt}f_{\rho_{t}}(x)
as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}[\sigma(u\cdot x)]

\displaystyle=-\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[\sigma^{\prime}(u\cdot x)x\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})\Big]\,.

(B.326)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}L(\rho_{t})

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))\frac{d}{dt}f_{\rho_{t}}(x)\Big]

(B.327)

\displaystyle=-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(u\cdot x)x\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})\Big]

(B.328)

\displaystyle=-\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})\cdot\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(u\cdot x)x]\Big]

(B.329)

\displaystyle=-\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})\cdot\nabla_{u}L(\rho_{t})\Big]

(B.330)

\displaystyle=-\E_{u\sim\rho_{t}}\Big[\|{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\,.

(B.c.
{\textup{grad}}_{u}=(I-uu^{\top})\nabla_{u}L(\rho_{t})
and
(I-uu^{\top})^{2}=(I-uu^{\top})
)

This completes the proof.
∎

The above lemma implies that
L(\rho_{t})
is always decreasing. Note that frequently, when we apply the above lemma, we will only consider the first coordinate of
{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho_{t})
.

Proposition B.36
.

For any distribution
\rho
on
\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\E_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho}[|w-w^{\prime}|^{2}]

\displaystyle=2\Var_{w\sim\rho}[w]

(B.331)

Proof of
Proposition

B.36
.

This follows from a short calculation:

\displaystyle\E_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho}[|w-w^{\prime}|^{2}]=\E_{w,w^{\prime}\sim\rho}(w^{2}-2ww^{\prime}+(w^{\prime})^{2})=2\E_{w\sim\rho}[w^{2}]-2(\E_{w\sim\rho}[w])^{2}=2\Var_{w\sim\rho}[w]

(B.332)

as desired.
∎

Appendix C

Proofs for Finite-Samples and Finite-Width Dynamics

In this section we give the proof for results related to the empirical dynamics, where the number of samples and network width are both polynomial in
d
. The end goal is to prove
Theorem

3.4
, the proof of which is given below.

Proof of
Theorem

3.4
.

We have

\displaystyle L({\hat{\rho}}_{T_{*,\epsilon}})

\displaystyle=\Exp_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{T_{*,\epsilon}}}(x)-y(x))^{2}]

(C.1)

\displaystyle\leq 3\Exp_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{T_{*,\epsilon}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{T_{*,\epsilon}}}(x))^{2}]+3\Exp_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{T_{*,\epsilon}}}(x)-f_{\rho_{T_{*,\epsilon}}}(x))^{2}]

(C.2)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+3\Exp_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{T_{*,\epsilon}}}(x)-y(x))^{2}]

(C.3)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\frac{1}{d^{\frac{\mu-1}{2}}}+\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}}{m}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon^{2}.

(C.4)

where the first inequality is by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the second inequality is by
Lemma

5.1
,
Lemma

5.2
and
Theorem

3.3
. Since
\epsilon=\frac{1}{\log\log d}
, the last term dominates because the conditions of
Theorem

3.4
imply that
m\gtrsim d^{3}
, and because
\frac{\mu-1}{2}>1
, meaning that the first term is at most
\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d}
.
∎

To complete the proof, we give proofs for results in
Section

5
.
We first give a proof for
Lemma

5.1
:

Proof of
Lemma

5.1
.

For the first statement, for any fixed
t\leq T
, by
Lemma

C.13
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(d^{2}+\log\frac{1}{\delta})(\log d)^{C}}{m}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.5)

with probability
1-\delta
— we will choose
\delta
shortly. In addition, for any
t,t^{\prime}\in[0,T]
, by
Lemma

C.14
, we have that
\|u_{t}(\chi)-u_{t^{\prime}}(\chi)\|_{2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}|t-t^{\prime}|
, which by
Lemma

C.12
implies that for any
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle|f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{\rho_{t^{\prime}}}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\E_{\chi\sim\rho_{0}}|\sigma(u_{t}(\chi)\cdot x)-\sigma(u_{t^{\prime}}(\chi)\cdot x)|

(C.6)

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}|t-t^{\prime}|

(C.7)

In particular, if
|t-t^{\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
, then for all
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have

\displaystyle|f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{\rho_{t^{\prime}}}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.8)

meaning that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{\rho_{t^{\prime}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.9)

By the same argument, we have that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t^{\prime}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.10)

Now, we perform a union bound over all times
t
such that
t\leq T
and
t
is an integer multiple of
\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{H\log d}}
for some sufficiently large absolute constant
H>0
. Since
T\leq\frac{d^{C}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}
, the number of such times is at most
d^{C+H\log d}
. Thus, with probability at least
1-\delta\cdot d^{C+H\log d}
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(d^{2}+\log\frac{1}{\delta})(\log d)^{O(1)}}{m}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.11)

for all
t\in[0,T]
. In particular, in order to have exponentially small failure probability, we set
\delta=\frac{1}{e^{d^{2}}d^{C+H\log d}}
, and by our assumption that
m\leq d^{O(\log d)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}}{m}

(C.12)

for all
t\leq T
, with probability at least
1-e^{-d^{2}}
, since
\log\frac{1}{\delta}=d^{2}+O((\log d)^{2})
