. This completes the proof of the first statement of the lemma. Additionally, by
Lemma

C.16
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\E_{(\bar{u},\hat{u})\sim\Gamma}[\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}]

(C.13)

which proves the second statement of the lemma.
∎

The rest of the section is dedicated to prove
Lemma

5.2
. To begin with, we first introduce the following lemma which formalizes the decomposition of
\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
discussed in
Section

5
.

Lemma C.1

(Decomposition of
\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Then,
\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=A_{t}+B_{t}+C_{t}
, where
A_{t}=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle
,
B_{t}=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle
, and
C_{t}=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.1
.

By the chain rule,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=2\Big\langle\frac{d}{dt}\hat{u}_{t}-\frac{d}{dt}\bar{u}_{t},\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\Big\rangle=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{{\hat{\rho}}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.14)

We can expand the difference in gradients as

\displaystyle{\textup{grad}}_{{\hat{\rho}}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t})

\displaystyle=\Big({\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})\Big)+\Big({\textup{grad}}L_{\hat{u}}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}L_{\hat{u}}(\rho_{t})\Big)

(C.15)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\Big({\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t})\Big)

(C.16)

Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle-2\langle{\textup{grad}}L_{\hat{u}}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}L_{\hat{u}}(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.17)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.18)

\displaystyle=C_{t}+B_{t}+A_{t}

(C.19)

as desired.
∎

Next, we give a proof of
Lemma

5.4
, which gives an upper bound for each of
A_{t}
,
B_{t}
and
C_{t}
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.4
.

The proof directly follows from
Lemma

C.8
,
Lemma

C.7
,
Lemma

C.9
,
Lemma

C.10
and
Lemma

C.11
.
∎

Combining the upper bounds in
Lemma

5.4
and the decomposition in
Lemma

C.1
, we have the following
Lemma

C.2
which upper bounds the growth of
\left\lVert\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\right\rVert
.
Recall that
T_{*,\epsilon}
is the total runtime for the algorithm (see
Theorem

3.3
), and
T_{1}
is the time such that
w_{T_{1}}(\iota_{\textup{U}})=\frac{1}{\log d}
(see
Definition

4.5
).

Lemma C.2

(Growth Rate of
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
With Dependency on
n
)
.

In the setting of
Theorem

3.4
, suppose that the inductive hypothesis in
5.3
holds for some
\phi
and
\psi
up until
t
for some
t\leq T_{*,\epsilon}
. Further assume that
\psi<\frac{1}{2}
and
\phi>\frac{1}{2}
. Then, when
t\leq T_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.20)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.21)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.22)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{d^{2-2\psi-\phi}}{n}+\frac{d^{4-4\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.23)

and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

(C.24)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}

(C.25)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}

(C.26)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}+\frac{d^{2-2\psi-\phi}}{n}+\frac{d^{4-4\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}\,.

(C.27)

When
t\in[T_{1},T_{*,\epsilon}]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.28)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.29)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.30)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{d^{2-2\psi-\phi}}{n}+\frac{d^{4-4\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.31)

and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

(C.32)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}

(C.33)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}

(C.34)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}+\frac{d^{2-2\psi-\phi}}{n}+\frac{d^{4-4\psi-2\phi}}{n}\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}\,.

(C.35)

Proof of
Lemma

C.2
.

This result follows directly from
Lemma

5.4
and the inductive hypothesis. By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\overline{\Delta}_{t}\leq d^{-\phi}
. We apply this to the bound for
C_{t}
in
Lemma

5.4
to obtain the desired bounds above. Note that in our bounds for
\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}
, it is important that we use the upper bound for
\E[B_{t}]
in
Lemma

5.4
instead of the upper bound for
B_{t}
.
∎

Using this bound on the growth rate, we can show that the inductive hypothesis is maintained.

Lemma C.3

(IH is Maintained)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Suppose
\psi>0
and
1>\phi>\frac{1}{2}+\psi
. Let
n=d^{\mu}(\log d)^{C}
where
\mu\geq\max\{2+2\phi,4-2\phi-4\psi\}
and
C
is a sufficiently large universal constant.
Suppose the inductive hypothesis in
5.3
holds for all
t\leq T
where
T<T_{*,\epsilon}
.
Then, when
m\gtrsim d^{3+2\phi}(\log d)^{\Omega(1)}
and
m\leq d^{C}
for a sufficiently large universal constant
C
, we have that the inductive hypothesis holds for all
t\leq T+c_{\textup{IH}}
where
c_{\textup{IH}}>0
is a constant that only depends on
d
,
\phi
,
\psi
,
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}
,
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}
.

Proof.

Suppose the inductive hypothesis holds for all
t\leq T
where
T\leq T_{1}
. Then, by
Lemma

C.2
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.36)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}}{d^{\phi}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}\Big)

(C.37)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}}{d^{\phi}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}\Big)\,.

(C.38)

Here we have used the fact that
n=d^{\mu}(\log d)^{O(1)}
where
\mu\geq\max(4-4\psi-2\phi,2+2\phi)
as well as the fact that
\phi>\frac{1}{2}
, as follows:

•

In the third term in the bound for
\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
from
Lemma

C.2
, only the
\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}
term is important — this term dominates the
\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}
and
\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}
terms, using the fact that
\mu>2+2\phi
.

•

In the fourth term in the bound for
\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
, the
\frac{d^{2-2\psi-\phi}}{n}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
term is dominated by the
\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}
term — this is because
\mu\geq 2+2\phi
.

•

Since
\mu\geq 4-4\psi-2\phi
, if we increase
n
by a
(\log d)^{O(1)}
factor, then the
\frac{d^{4-4\psi-2\phi}}{n}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
term can be absorbed into the first term, as it is dominated by the
4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
term. (Recall that by
Lemma

B.7
,
|D_{2,t}|\gtrsim\gamma_{2}
during Phase 1).

In the second inequality above, we have used the fact that
m\gtrsim d^{3+2\phi}(\log d)^{\Omega(1)}
— this implies that the term involving
m
is dominated by the
\frac{(\log d)^{O(1)}}{d^{\phi}}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}
term.

Additionally, by
Lemma

C.2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}+\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}

(C.39)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})}{d^{0.5+\phi}(\log d)^{O(1)}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)

(C.40)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)\Big)\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}+O\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})}{d^{0.5+\phi}(\log d)^{O(1)}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)\,.

(C.41)

Here, we apply the bound for
\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}
from
Lemma

C.2
. In the third term in this bound, the
\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}
term dominates the
\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}
term, using the fact that
\phi>\frac{1}{2}
and
\mu\geq 2+2\phi>3
, meaning
\frac{d^{2.5-2\psi-2\phi}}{n}\leq\frac{d^{1.5}}{n}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
. In the fourth term, we deal with the
\frac{d^{4-4\psi-2\phi}}{n}
term similarly to our calculations for the bound for
\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. We can also deal with the
\frac{d^{2-2\psi-\phi}}{n}
term similarly, since by our definition of
\mu
, this will be at most
\frac{1}{d^{3\phi}}\leq\frac{1}{(\log d)^{O(1)}}
, and will thus be absorbed into the first term involving
|D_{2,t}|
. Using
\mu\geq 2+2\phi
, we then obtain
\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{0.5+\phi}}
as the coefficient of the
\overline{\Delta}_{t}
term (i.e. the first-order term involving
\overline{\Delta}_{t}
). Note that here we need to have
m\gtrsim d^{3+2\phi}(\log d)^{\Omega(1)}
in order to absorb the term involving
m
into the
\frac{1}{d^{0.5+\phi}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}
term.

Now using
Lemma

G.12
we have

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\log d)^{O(1)}}\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})O(\frac{1}{d^{0.5+\phi}})}{4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\min_{t}|D_{2,t}|}\Big)^{2}\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{T}4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)dt\Big)

(C.42)

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{\textup{max},T}^{2}\lesssim(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})O(\frac{1}{d^{\phi}})}{4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\min_{t}|D_{2,t}|}\Big)^{2}\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{T}4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\Big)dt\Big)

(C.43)

By
Lemma

4.8
we have

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\int_{t=0}^{T}4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|dt\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim d

(C.44)

and by
Lemma

B.7
we know that
|D_{2,t}|\gtrsim\gamma_{2}
during Phase 1. Thus,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\textup{max},T}^{2}\leq O\Big(\frac{(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}d^{2\phi-1}}\Big).

(C.45)

Since
2\phi-1>2\psi
and
d
is chosen to be sufficiently large after
\gamma_{2}
is chosen (
3.2
), we have that
\Delta_{\textup{max},T}<\sqrt{\frac{1}{d^{\psi+\phi-1/2}}}=\frac{1}{d^{\frac{1}{2}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\psi-\frac{1}{4}}}<\frac{1}{d^{\psi}}
. Note that
\Delta_{\textup{max},t}
is continuous in
t
and we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Delta_{\textup{max},t}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\chi}\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.46)

\displaystyle=\max_{\chi}\frac{1}{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}}\frac{d}{dt}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.47)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\chi}\frac{1}{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}}|\langle\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t},{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}(\hat{u}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}_{t}}L(\bar{u}_{t})\rangle|

(C.48)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\chi}\|{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}(\hat{u}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}_{t}}L(\bar{u}_{t})\|_{2}

(By Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality)

\displaystyle\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}\,.

(By
Lemma

C.14
)

Thus, for
\mu_{1}=\Big(\frac{1}{d^{\psi}}-\frac{1}{d^{\frac{1}{2}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\psi-\frac{1}{4}}}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}}
we still have the inductive hypothesis for
\Delta_{\textup{max}}
until time
T+\mu_{1}
, that is,
\Delta_{\textup{max},T+\mu_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{\psi}}
. Similarly, our calculation above using
Lemma

G.12
gives us

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{2}d^{2\phi}(\log d)^{O(1)}}\Big)

(C.49)

Thus, we have
\overline{\Delta}_{T}<\frac{1}{d^{\phi}(\log d)^{O(1)}}<\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}
(where in the middle expression, we choose a smaller power of
(\log d)^{O(1)}
so that the inequality holds). Observe that
\overline{\Delta}_{t}
is continuous in
t
, and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\Delta}_{t}}\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

(C.50)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\Delta}_{t}}\E_{\chi}[\langle\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t},\hat{u}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime}\rangle]

(C.51)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\overline{\Delta}_{t}}\E_{\chi}[\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}\|\hat{u}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}]

(By Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}}{\overline{\Delta}_{t}}\E_{\chi}[\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}]

(By
Lemma

C.14
)

\displaystyle\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}\,.

(C.52)

Thus, for
\mu_{2}=\Big(\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}-\frac{1}{d^{\phi}(\log d)^{O(1)}}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}}
we still have the inductive hypothesis for
\overline{\Delta}
until time
T+\mu_{2}
, i.e.
\overline{\Delta}_{T+\mu_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{\phi}}
. As a result, assuming the inductive hypothesis holds up to time
T
, it holds up to time
T+\min(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
.

Our above proof strategy applies to all times
T\leq T_{1}
. For the induction when
T\in[T_{1},T_{*,\epsilon}]
we follow a similar argument but instead use the bound on
A_{t}
obtained from
Lemma

C.8
. Since we know from
Lemma

B.32
and the assumption that
\epsilon=O(\frac{1}{\log\log d})
that

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{*,\epsilon}}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}dt\leq(T_{*,\epsilon}-T_{1})(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\leq\frac{\textup{poly}(\log\log d)}{\gamma_{2}^{O(1)}},

(C.53)

we know that this additional phase will at most add another
\exp(\textup{poly}(\log\log d))
factor to the growth of
\Delta_{\textup{max},t}
which is smaller than any polynomial in
d
. Thus,
Equation

C.45
and
Equation

C.49
still hold for this period, which finishes the proof.
∎

Finally, we prove
Lemma

5.2
:

Proof of
Lemma

5.2
.

The proof directly follows
Lemma

C.3
with
\phi=\frac{\mu-1}{4}
and
\psi=\frac{\mu-3}{8}
.
∎

C.1

Upper bound on
A_{t}

The goal of this section is to obtain an upper bound on

\displaystyle A_{t}(\chi):=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}(\chi)-\bar{u}_{t}(\chi)\rangle\,.

(C.54)

When there is no risk of confusion, we simply use
A_{t}
,
\hat{u}_{t}
, and
\bar{u}_{t}
, omitting the dependence on
\chi
.

We first obtain a strong upper bound on this inner product during Phase 1 of the population projected gradient flow. To obtain an upper bound on this inner product, we separately consider the contributions from the first coordinates of the vectors, and the remaining
(d-1)
coordinates — in other words, the gradients with respect to
w
and the gradients with respect to
z
. For convenience, let
{\textup{grad}}_{w}L(\rho)
denote the gradient with respect to
w
and
{\textup{grad}}_{z}L(\rho)
denote the gradient with respect to
z
. The following lemma is about the contribution of the first coordinates of the particles to
A_{t}
, and is useful during Phase 1.

Lemma C.4

(Contribution of
w
’s)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
, and let
\rho_{t}
be the projected gradient flow on population loss, initialized with the uniform distribution on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, defined in
Equation

3.7
. Suppose
D_{2,t}<0
and
D_{4,t}<0
, where
D_{2,t}
and
D_{4,t}
are as defined in
Section

4.1
. Then, the contribution of the first coordinates to
A_{t}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle-2\Big({\textup{grad}}_{\hat{w}}L(\rho_{t})

\displaystyle-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})\Big)\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

(C.55)

\displaystyle\leq 4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+O(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|{w_{\textup{max}}}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2})

(C.56)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|\Delta_{\textup{max}}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}\Big)

(C.57)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}\Big)\,.

(C.58)

where
{w_{\textup{max}}}
is as defined in
Definition

4.5
.

The constant
4
at the beginning of the first term is particularly important since it determines the growth rate of
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
, and in turn affects the number of samples we will need. At least during Phase 1, the contribution of the first coordinates will be the dominant term in
A_{t}
, as we will see in the next few lemmas.

Proof of
Lemma

C.4
.

The contribution of the first coordinate to the dot product defining
A_{t}
is

\displaystyle-2\Big({\textup{grad}}_{\hat{w}}L(\rho_{t})-

\displaystyle{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})\Big)\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

(C.59)

\displaystyle=2(v(\hat{w}_{t})-v(\bar{w}_{t}))\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

(C.60)

where
v(w)
is as defined in
Lemma

4.4
. We can further simplify
v(\hat{w}_{t})-v(\bar{w}_{t})
using
Lemma

4.4
. Let
P_{t}
and
Q_{t}
be defined as in
Lemma

4.4
. Then,

\displaystyle v(\hat{w}_{t})-v(\bar{w}_{t})

\displaystyle=-(1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2})(P_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})+Q_{t}(\hat{w}_{t}))+(1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2})(P_{t}(\bar{w}_{t})+Q_{t}(\bar{w}_{t}))\,.

(C.61)

Let us upper bound the term corresponding to
Q_{t}
first. Since each coefficient of
Q_{t}
is bounded above by
O(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d})
(by the conclusion of
Lemma

4.4
) and
Q_{t}
only has linear and cubic terms, we have

\displaystyle\Big|-(1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2})Q_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})+(1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2})Q_{t}(\bar{w}_{t})\Big|\cdot|\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}|\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t}|^{2}

(C.62)

by
Lemma

G.16
. The terms corresponding to
P_{t}(\bar{w}_{t})
in
(v(\hat{w}_{t})-v(\bar{w}_{t}))(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})
can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle(-

\displaystyle(1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2})P_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})+(1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2})P_{t}(\bar{w}_{t}))\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

(C.63)

\displaystyle=2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}(\hat{w}_{t}^{2}+\hat{w}_{t}\bar{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}^{2})

(C.64)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}(\hat{w}_{t}^{2}+\hat{w}_{t}\bar{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}^{2})-4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}(\hat{w}_{t}^{2}+\hat{w}_{t}\bar{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}^{2})

(C.65)

\displaystyle\leq 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}(\hat{w}_{t}^{2}+\hat{w}_{t}\bar{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}^{2})

(Since last two terms are nonnegative)

\displaystyle\leq 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+O(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}||\hat{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2})

(Since
\bar{w}_{t},\hat{w}_{t}\leq 1
)

Thus, the overall bound on the contribution of the first coordinates to
A_{t}
is

\displaystyle 2(v(\hat{w}_{t})-v(\bar{w}_{t}))\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot(-(1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2})P_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})+(1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2})P_{t}(\bar{w}_{t}))\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

(C.66)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\Big|-(1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2})Q_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})+(1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2})Q_{t}(\bar{w}_{t})\Big|\cdot|\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t}|

(C.67)

\displaystyle\leq 4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+O(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}||\hat{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2})

(C.68)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}\Big)

(C.69)

We can simplify this bound further in terms of
{w_{\textup{max}}}
and
\Delta_{\textup{max},t}
. First observe that

\displaystyle|\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t}|\leq\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}\leq\Delta_{\textup{max},t}

(C.70)

Thus, by the triangle inequality, we have
|\bar{w}_{t}|\leq{w_{\textup{max}}}
and
|\hat{w}_{t}|\leq{w_{\textup{max}}}+\Delta_{\textup{max},t}
meaning

\displaystyle 2(v(\hat{w}_{t})-v(\bar{w}_{t}))\cdot(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq 4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+O(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}||\hat{w}_{t}+\bar{w}_{t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2})

(C.71)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}\Big)

(C.72)

\displaystyle\leq 4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+O(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|{w_{\textup{max}}}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2})

(C.73)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|\Delta_{\textup{max},t}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}\Big)

(C.74)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}\Big)

(C.75)

as desired.
∎

Next, we study the contribution of the last
(d-1)
coordinates of the particles to
A_{t}(\chi)
during Phase 1. We first introduce the following formula for the projected gradient of the population loss with respect to the last
(d-1)
coordinates of a particle.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
\rho
be a distribution which is rotationally invariant, as in
Definition

4.1
. Let
{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho)
be defined as in
Equation

3.4
. In addition, let
{\textup{grad}}_{z}L(\rho)
denote the last
(d-1)
coordinates of
{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho)
, for any particle
u=(w,z)
. Then, for any particle
u=(w,z)
, we have
{\textup{grad}}_{z}L(\rho)=-\Big(\frac{w}{1-w^{2}}\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{w}L(\rho)\Big)z
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.5
.

Notice that
w^{2}+z^{2}=1
for any
u=(w,z)\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and this equality is maintained by projected gradient descent on the population loss, as in
Equation

3.7
. Also, by
Lemma

G.15
, we have
{\textup{grad}}_{z}L(\rho)=cz
for some scalar
c
, since
\rho
is rotationally invariant. Thus,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}(w^{2}+z^{2})

(C.77)

\displaystyle=-2w{\textup{grad}}_{w}L(\rho)-2\langle z,{\textup{grad}}_{z}L(\rho)\rangle

(C.78)

\displaystyle=-2w{\textup{grad}}_{w}L(\rho)-2c\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}

(C.79)

\displaystyle=-2w{\textup{grad}}_{w}L(\rho)-2c(1-w^{2}).

(C.80)

Rearranging gives us

\displaystyle c=-\frac{w}{1-w^{2}}{\textup{grad}}_{w}L(\rho).

(C.81)

∎

By directly applying this formula for the last
(d-1)
coordinates of the projected gradient, we have the following upper bound on the contribution of the last
(d-1)
coordinates to
A_{t}
during Phase 1.

Lemma C.6

(Contribution of
z
’s During Phase 1.)
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Let
t\leq T_{1}
(where
T_{1}
is as defined in
Definition

4.5
). Let
\rho_{t}
be defined according to the population projected gradient flow as in
Equation

3.7
. Let
{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho)
be as in
Equation

3.4
, and let
{\textup{grad}}_{z}L(\rho)
denote the last
(d-1)
coordinates of
{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho)
for any particle
u=(w,z)
. Then, we have the following upper bound:

\displaystyle-2\big\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{z}}L(\rho_{t})-

\displaystyle{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{z}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{z}_{t}-\bar{z}_{t}\big\rangle

(C.82)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(|\bar{w}_{t}|+|\hat{w}_{t}|)\left\lVert\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|(|\bar{w}_{t}^{3}|+|\hat{w}_{t}^{3}|)\left\lVert\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

(C.83)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\|\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(C.84)

Proof of
Lemma

C.6
.

Using the formula for projected gradient in
Lemma

C.5
, we have that

\displaystyle-\big\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{z}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{z}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{z}_{t}-\bar{z}_{t}\big\rangle

(C.85)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,=\Big\langle\frac{\hat{w}_{t}}{1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2}}\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{w}}L(\rho_{t})\cdot\hat{z}_{t}-\frac{\bar{w}_{t}}{1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}}\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})\cdot\bar{z}_{t},\hat{z}_{t}-\bar{z}_{t}\Big\rangle

(C.86)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,=\Big(\frac{\hat{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\bar{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\hat{z}^{\top}(\hat{z}_{t}-\bar{z}_{t})+\frac{\bar{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}}\|\hat{z}_{t}-\bar{z}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.87)

The second term is always negative for
t\leq T_{1}
, because
Lemma

B.8
implies that
v(\bar{w}_{t})
has the same sign as
\bar{w}_{t}
, meaning that
{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})
has the opposite sign. We bound the first term as

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\hat{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\bar{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\hat{z}_{t}^{\top}(\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t})

(C.88)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\Big|\frac{\hat{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\hat{w}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{\bar{w}_{t}{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{w}}L(\rho_{t})}{1-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}}\Big|\cdot\left\lVert\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\right\rVert

(B.c.
|\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}=1
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,=|\hat{w}_{t}(P_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})+Q_{t}(\hat{w}_{t}))-\bar{w}_{t}(P_{t}(\bar{w}_{t})+Q_{t}(\bar{w}_{t}))|\cdot\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}

(By
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}||\hat{w}_{t}^{2}-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}|\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}||\hat{w}_{t}^{4}-\bar{w}_{t}^{4}|\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.89)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+|Q_{t}(\bar{w}_{t})-Q_{t}(\hat{w}_{t})|\cdot\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}

(By
Lemma

4.4
and Def. of
P_{t}
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq 2\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}||\hat{w}_{t}^{2}-\bar{w}_{t}^{2}|\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}+4\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}||\hat{w}_{t}^{4}-\bar{w}_{t}^{4}|\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}

(C.90)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)|\bar{w}_{t}-\hat{w}_{t}|\|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}

(By
Lemma

G.16
and Def. of
Q_{t}
from
Lemma

4.4
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(|\bar{w}_{t}|+|\hat{w}_{t}|)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|(|\bar{w}_{t}|^{3}+|\hat{w}_{t}|^{3})\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.91)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(B.c.
|\bar{w}_{t}-\hat{w}_{t}|\leq\|\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}
and
|\bar{z}_{t}-\hat{z}_{t}\|_{2}\leq\|\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}
)

as desired.
∎

We can combine
Lemma

C.4
and
Lemma

C.6
and get the following bound on
A_{t}
during Phase 1.

Lemma C.7
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Let
t\leq T_{1}
(where
T_{1}
is as defined in
Definition

4.5
). Then,

\displaystyle A_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 4\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|(\hat{w}_{t}-\bar{w}_{t})^{2}+O((\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|){w_{\textup{max}}})\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.92)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big((\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|)\Delta_{\textup{max}}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.93)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\Big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}|D_{2,t}|+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}|D_{4,t}|}{d}\Big)\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(C.94)

Proof of
Lemma

C.7
.

This directly follows from
Lemma

C.4
and
Lemma

C.6
.
∎

Next, we give a potentially looser bound for
A_{t}
which holds even after Phase 1. The running time after Phase 1 is at most
(\log\log d)^{O(1})
(aside from the dependency on
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}
, etc.), meaning that even the following growth rate is enough to ensure that the coupling error grows by only a sub-polynomial factor after Phase 1.

Lemma C.8
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Then, for all
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|A_{t}|\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\left\lVert\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}.

(C.95)

Proof of
Lemma

C.8
.

We first consider the part of
A_{t}
that originates from the non-projected gradients, which is defined as

\displaystyle A_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=-2\langle\nabla_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.96)

\displaystyle=-2\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\big[(f_{\rho}(x)-y(x))(\sigma^{\prime}(\bar{u}_{t}^{\top}x)-\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x))(\bar{u}_{t}^{\top}x-\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x)\big].

(C.97)

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle|A_{t}^{\prime}|\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-y(x))^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\sigma^{\prime}(\bar{u}_{t}^{\top}x)-\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x))^{2}(\bar{u}_{t}^{\top}x-\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(C.98)

We bound the first factor in the equation above by the loss at initialization, which is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{0}}(x)-y)^{2}]\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}^{2}.

(C.99)

by
Lemma

B.7
. Thus, plugging
Equation

C.99
and
Equation

C.208
(from the statement of
Lemma

C.15
) into
Equation

C.98
gives us

\displaystyle|A_{t}^{\prime}|\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\left\lVert\delta_{t}\right\rVert^{2}.

(C.100)

Next, we bound the difference between
A_{t}
and
A_{t}^{\prime}
. We have

\displaystyle A_{t}-A_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-\nabla_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.101)

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle-\langle\nabla_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.102)

For the first term of
Equation

C.102
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.103)

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),(\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})\hat{u}_{t}-(\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.104)

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot(1-\langle\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t}\rangle)

(C.105)

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\frac{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}

(B.c.
\|\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}=\|\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}=1
)

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x)\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x]\cdot\frac{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}

(C.106)

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x)\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x]\cdot\frac{\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}

(C.107)

and by an argument similar to the one used to bound
A^{\prime}_{t}
(here using
Equation

C.209
from
Lemma

C.15
instead of
Equation

C.208
), we can thus show that

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\rangle|\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\left\lVert\delta_{t}\right\rVert^{2}.

(C.108)

Similarly, for the second term we have

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\bar{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t}\rangle|\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\gamma_{2}\left\lVert\delta_{t}\right\rVert^{2}.

(C.109)

Combining
Equation

C.108
,
Equation

C.109
and
Equation

C.100
finishes the proof.
∎

C.2

Upper Bound on
B_{t}

The goal of this section is to obtain an upper bound on

\displaystyle B_{t}(\chi):=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}(\chi)-\bar{u}_{t}(\chi)\rangle.

(C.110)

When there is no risk of confusion, we simply use
B_{t}
,
\hat{u}_{t}
, and
\bar{u}_{t}
, omitting the
\chi
dependency. In the next lemma, we first obtain a bound on
B_{t}(\chi)
, and after that, we obtain a bound on
\E_{\chi}[B_{t}(\chi)]
. The proof of the following lemma is mostly a straightforward calculation, and follows mostly from
Lemma

5.1
and the definition of
\overline{\Delta}
.

Lemma C.9
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Suppose
T\lesssim\frac{d^{C}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}
for any absolute constant
C
. Assume the width
m
of
{\bar{\rho}},{\hat{\rho}}
is at most
d^{O(\log d)}
. Then, with probability at least
1-e^{-d^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}}{m}

(C.111)

for all times
t\in[0,T]
. Additionally, with probability at least
1-e^{-d^{2}}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\chi\in{\hat{\rho}}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle B_{t}(\chi)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\Big(\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}+\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}\,.

(C.112)

Proof of
Lemma

C.9
.

We first decompose
B_{t}
into two terms:

\displaystyle B_{t}

\displaystyle=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.113)

\displaystyle=-2\langle(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.114)

\displaystyle=-2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})(\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t})\rangle

(C.115)

\displaystyle=-2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle+2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}(\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t})\rangle

(C.116)

\displaystyle=-2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle+2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot(1-\langle\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t}\rangle)

(C.117)

\displaystyle=-2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle+2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\frac{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}

(C.118)

where the last equality is because
\frac{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}=1-\langle\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t}\rangle
since
\|\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}=\|\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}=1
. For convenience, define
\delta_{t}:=\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle B_{t}=-2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\delta_{t}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.119)

We first focus on the first term in
Equation

C.119
, which is the main contributor to
B_{t}
.

Analysis of First Term in
Equation

C.119
.

For convenience, we refer to the first term in
Equation

C.119
as
B_{t}^{\prime}
. Observe that we can expand
B_{t}^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle B_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-2\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\delta_{t}\rangle

(C.120)

\displaystyle=-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]

(By
Equation

3.4
)

\displaystyle=-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]

(C.121)

Let us bound the first term of
Equation

C.121
. By
Lemma

5.1
, with probability
e^{-d^{2}}
, for all
t\leq T
we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho_{t}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}_{t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}}{m}\,.

(C.122)

Thus, we can bound the first term of
Equation

C.121
by

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.123)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))^{2}]^{1/2}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

(By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))^{2}]^{1/2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(By
Lemma

C.15
,
Equation

C.209
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}\cdot d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(By
Equation

C.122
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}\,.

(C.124)

Next, we bound the second term in
Equation

C.121
. By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.125)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]^{1/2}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

(C.126)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\overline{\Delta}_{t}\cdot\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

(By
Lemma

5.1
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\overline{\Delta}_{t}\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(By
Lemma

C.15
(
Equation

C.209
))

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(C.127)

Combining
Equation

C.124
and
Equation

C.127
with
Equation

C.121
, we find that

\displaystyle|B_{t}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\Big(\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}+\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}\,.

(C.128)

Analysis of Second Term in
Equation

C.119
.

For convenience, we write this term as
B_{t}^{\prime\prime}
, i.e. we define
B_{t}^{\prime\prime}:=\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. It suffices to show that the first dot product in the definition of
B_{t}^{\prime\prime}
is at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})
up to constant factors. To start, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.129)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.130)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.131)

Both terms in
Equation

C.131
can be bounded as before. For the first term we use
Equation

C.122
:

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.132)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]^{1/2}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

(By Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\,.

(By
Equation

C.122
and
Lemma

C.15
,
Equation

C.209
)

For the second term, we use
Lemma

C.16
:

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

(C.133)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]^{1/2}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle^{2}]^{1/2}

(By Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}

(By
Lemma

C.16
and
Lemma

C.15
,
Equation

C.209
)

Thus, we can conclude that

\displaystyle|B_{t}^{\prime\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\Big(\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}+\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)\cdot\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.134)

Overall Bound on
B_{t}
.

Combining our bounds on
B_{t}^{\prime}
and
B_{t}^{\prime\prime}
, and using the fact that
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}\leq 2
to absorb the bound for
B_{t}^{\prime\prime}
into the bound for
B_{t}^{\prime}
, we find that

\displaystyle|B_{t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\Big(\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}+\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(C.135)

with probability
1-e^{-d^{2}}
(recall that
Equation

C.122
held with probability
1-e^{-d^{2}}
). This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we show a tighter bound when we take the expectation of
B_{t}
over the neurons, i.e. over
\chi
. Taking the expectation allows us to obtain a tighter bound because of the term

\displaystyle-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]\,.

(C.136)

For a single
\hat{u}_{t}
, the best bound we can obtain is by using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. However, the key point in the following lemma is that when we take the expectation over
(\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t})\sim\Gamma_{t}
, this term becomes

\displaystyle-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]\leq 0

(C.137)

together with a third-order term which is at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}
, which is a significantly better bound than
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}
, which we obtained in the previous lemma. This is because to simplify
Equation

C.136
, we can use

\displaystyle\sigma(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sigma(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\bar{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle+O(\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2})

(C.138)

meaning that we can essentially substitute
\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle\approx\sigma(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle)
in
Equation

C.136
. Note that we need this better bound on the growth rate of
\overline{\Delta}_{t}
since our inductive hypothesis imposes a stricter condition on
\overline{\Delta}_{t}
.

Lemma C.10
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Lemma

C.9
, and in particular, suppose
T
satisfies the same assumptions as in the statement of
Lemma

C.9
. Then, for all
t\leq T
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{\chi\sim{\hat{\rho}}_{0}}[B_{t}(\chi)]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}\,.

(C.139)

Proof of
Lemma

C.10
.

We again make use of
Equation

C.119
, and we recall from the proof of the previous lemma that
B_{t}=B_{t}^{\prime}+B_{t}^{\prime\prime}
, where

\displaystyle B_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-f_{\rho}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]

(C.140)

and

\displaystyle B_{t}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-\nabla_{\hat{u}}L(\rho_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(C.141)

The main difference between
Lemma

C.9
is that we now bound the second term of
B_{t}^{\prime}
in expectation. By Taylor’s theorem, we can write

\displaystyle\sigma(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sigma(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle-\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)+\frac{\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}{2}(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle-\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}

(C.142)

for some
\lambda
which is a convex combination of
\langle\hat{u},x\rangle
and
\langle\bar{u},x\rangle
. Rearranging gives

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle=\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle=\sigma(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle)+\frac{\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)}{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}

(C.143)

Thus, taking the expectation of the second term of
B_{t}^{\prime}
over
(\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t})\sim\Gamma_{t}
gives

\displaystyle-2\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]

(C.144)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,=-2\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))(\sigma(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle))]

(C.145)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]

(By
Equation

C.143
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,=-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

(C.146)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]

(By
Equation

3.1
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq-\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]

(C.147)

Next, let us bound the inner expectation in
Equation

C.147
. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{2}]\Big|

(C.148)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]^{1/2}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}

(C.149)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\overline{\Delta}_{t}\cdot\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}

(By
Lemma

C.16
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\overline{\Delta}_{t}\cdot\Big(\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}+\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}\Big)

(C.150)

Here to obtain the last inequality, we observe that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
is convex because
{P_{2,d}}^{\prime\prime}
is a constant function and
{P_{4,d}}^{\prime\prime}
is a quadratic function by
Equation

A.4
— additionally, we used the inequality
(a+b)^{2}\lesssim a^{2}+b^{2}
and the fact that
\lambda
is a convex combination of
\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle
and
\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle
.

By an argument similar to the proof of
Lemma

C.15
, we can show that
\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle)^{2}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{1/2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. Combining this with
Equation

C.147
, we have that

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.151)

and thus,

\displaystyle-2\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}

(C.152)

by the definition of
\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}
. This gives a bound on the second term of
B_{t}^{\prime}
. Combining this with
Equation

C.124
to bound the first term of
B_{t}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}[B_{t}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}

(C.153)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}

(By Cauchy-Schwarz)

Combining this with
Equation

C.134
(for which we also take the expectation over
(\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t})\sim\Gamma_{t}
), we have that

\displaystyle\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}|B_{t}^{\prime\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\cdot\Big(\frac{d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}+\overline{\Delta}_{t}\Big)\cdot\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{2}

(C.154)

which is at most the bound that we have for
B_{t}^{\prime}
, and thus,

\displaystyle\E_{(\hat{u},\bar{u})\sim\Gamma_{t}}[B_{t}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d(\log d)^{O(1)}}{\sqrt{m}}\overline{\Delta}_{t}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{t}^{3}

(C.155)

as desired.
∎

C.3

Upper Bound on
C_{t}

The goal of this section is to obtain an upper bound on

\displaystyle C_{t}(\chi):=-2\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}(\chi)-\bar{u}_{t}(\chi)\rangle\,.

(C.156)

This quantity is essentially the part of the growth of
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
which is due to the difference between the gradient using finite samples and the gradient using infinitely many samples. When there is no risk of confusion, we simply use
C_{t}
,
\hat{u}_{t}
, and
\bar{u}_{t}
, omitting the
\chi
dependency.
We bound this term with the following lemma:

Lemma C.11
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.4
. Assume that the number of samples
x_{i}
is
n\leq d^{C}
, for any universal constant
C>0
. Assume that the width
m\leq d^{C}
for any universal constant
C>0
. Let
T>0
. Assume for all
t\in[0,T]
we have a bound
B_{1}
on
\overline{\Delta}_{t}
with
B_{1}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. Additionally, assume for all
t\in[0,T]
that we have a bound
B_{2}
on
\Delta_{\textup{max},t}
such that
B_{2}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. Then, with probability
1-\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\chi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle|C_{t}(\chi)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}\cdot\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{2}}{n}B_{2}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{d^{2.5}}{n}\overline{\Delta}^{2}B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{4}}{n}B_{2}^{4}\overline{\Delta}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\,.

(C.157)

where
\delta_{t}=\hat{u}_{t}(\chi)-\bar{u}_{t}(\chi)
.

Proof.

We bound
C_{t}
by expanding
{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})
as the sum of several monomials, as well as expanding
{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})
, and then applying
Lemma

G.7
to each term from
{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{t})
and the corresponding term from
{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})
. For convenience, we use
\ell^{(x)}({\hat{\rho}})
to denote the loss of
f_{{\hat{\rho}}}
on a single data point
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\ell^{(x)}({\hat{\rho}})
to denote the projected gradient of
\ell^{(x)}({\hat{\rho}})
with respect to
\hat{u}
. We have

\displaystyle\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\ell^{(x)}({\hat{\rho}}_{t}),\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)x,(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})(\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t})\rangle

(C.158)

\displaystyle=\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)x,\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.159)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)x,\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot(1-\langle\hat{u}_{t},\bar{u}_{t}\rangle)

(C.160)

\displaystyle=\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)x,\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\rangle

(C.161)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)x,\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\frac{\|\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}

(C.162)

For convenience, define
\delta_{t}:=\hat{u}_{t}-\bar{u}_{t}
. Then we can rewrite the above as

\displaystyle\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\ell^{(x)}({\hat{\rho}}_{t}),\delta_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)\langle x,\delta_{t}\rangle

(C.163)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\langle(f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)x,\hat{u}_{t}\rangle\cdot\frac{\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{2}

(C.164)

For illustration, define
\mathcal{C}(g)
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(g):=\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i})-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[g(x)]\Big|

(C.165)

i.e. how close the empirical mean of the
g(x_{i})
is to its expected value. We will show that
\mathcal{C}(g)
is small when
g
corresponds to each of the terms on the right-hand side of
Equation

C.163
, even when we take the supremum over the
\hat{u}_{t}
, which may depend on the
x_{i}
.

Concentration for Mean of First Term in
Equation

C.163
.

As a first step, we list all of the terms we obtain when expanding the first term on the right-hand side of
Equation

C.163
. The
\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)
factor consists of the following terms: (1) a
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}\sqrt{N_{2,d}}\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle
term, (2) a
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle^{3}
term, and (3) a
\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}}{d}\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle
term — here, we have stated these terms up to absolute constant factors for convenience. In the
f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)-y(x)
factor,
f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)
contributes (1) a
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}\sqrt{N_{2,d}}\langle\hat{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{2}
term, (2) a
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}\langle\hat{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{4}
term, and (3) a
\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}}{d}\langle\hat{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{2}
term. Here note that
\hat{u}_{t}^{\prime}
is not a single vector, but ranges over the entire support of
{\hat{\rho}}_{t}
. Thus we implicitly perform a union bound over the
m
vectors in the support of
{\hat{\rho}}_{t}
— this does not affect the proof since the failure probability of
Lemma

G.7
is
\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
, while we assume the width
m
is at most
d^{C}
where
C
can be any absolute constant. Additionally,
y(x)
consists of the following terms: (1) a
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}\gamma_{2}\sqrt{N_{2,d}}\langle e_{1},x\rangle^{2}
term, (2) a
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\gamma_{4}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}\langle e_{1},x\rangle^{4}
term, and (3) a
\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\gamma_{4}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}}{d}\langle e_{1},x\rangle^{2}
term. Finally, there is a factor of
\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle
.

For convenience, throughout this proof, let
\bar{\delta}_{t}=\frac{\delta_{t}}{\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}}
. In each of the above factors, for every occurrence of
\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle
, we substitute
\hat{u}_{t}=\bar{u}_{t}+\delta_{t}
and further expand. This is useful since originally
\hat{u}_{t}
depends on the
x_{i}
and thus, when we applied
Lemma

G.7
we would have to take the supremum over
\hat{u}_{t}
— however, now
\bar{u}_{t}
can be considered a fixed vector with respect to the
x_{i}
, and while we need to take the supremum over
\delta_{t}
, in each term where
\delta_{t}
occurs, we obtain an additional factor of
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}
, which reduces the error from uniform convergence. We now completely expand the first term on the right-hand side of
Equation

C.163
. First we consider the contribution to
\mathcal{C}(g)
of
f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)
and later study
y(x)
. Up to constant factors, it suffices to obtain an upper bound on the concentration of

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+r=1,3\\
q+s=2,4\end{subarray}}\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}d^{\frac{p+1+q+r+s}{2}}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{p+1}\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{q}\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle^{r}\langle\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{s}

(C.166)

where we have let
\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}=\max(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|,|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|)
. Here, the sum ranges over
p+q=1,3
and
q+s=2,4
since the
\delta_{t}
and
\bar{u}_{t}
terms are due to the
\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\hat{u}_{t},x\rangle)
factor, while the
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
and
\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime}
terms are due to the
f_{{\hat{\rho}}}(x)
factors.

We now obtain concentration bounds for each of the terms in
Equation

C.166
. First, we consider the case where
p+q\geq 1
, as in this case,
Lemma

G.7
can be applied (since we can choose
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4}
in
Lemma

G.7
so that
u_{1}=\bar{\delta}_{t}
and
p_{1}=p+1
, and
u_{2}=\bar{\delta}_{t}^{\prime}
and
p_{2}=q
, and
p_{3}=p_{4}=0
— the hypotheses are then satisfied since
p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4}\geq 2
). From
Lemma

G.7
, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}d^{\frac{p+q+r+s+1}{2}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{p+1}\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{q}\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle^{r}\langle\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{s}-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{p+1}\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{q}\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle^{r}\langle\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{s}]\Big|

(C.167)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{\frac{p+1+q}{2}}}{n}\Big)\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}

(C.168)

Here in applying
Lemma

G.7
, we have assumed that the number of samples
n
is at most
d^{C}
for some absolute constant
C
, meaning that the
\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
term in the conclusion is a lower-order term. Lastly, we consider the case where
p+q=0
. To apply
Lemma

G.7
, we can let
u_{1}=\bar{u}_{t}
,
p_{1}=1
,
u_{2}=\frac{\delta_{t}}{\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}}
,
p_{2}=1
, and let
p_{3}=p_{4}=0
, with
w_{1}=\bar{u}_{t}
,
q_{1}=r-1
and
w_{2}=\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime}
,
q_{2}=s
— since
p_{1}+p_{2}=2
, the hypotheses of
Lemma

G.7
are still satisfied. This leads to us obtaining only one power of
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}
in the bound for this term: if
p=q=0
, then we have the bound

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}d^{\frac{p+q+r+s+1}{2}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{p+1}\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{q}\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle^{r}\langle\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{s}-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{p+1}\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{q}\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle^{r}\langle\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{s}]\Big|

(C.169)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d}{n}\Big)\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(C.170)

Summing over the possible values of
p,q,r,s
, we find that the overall contribution to the concentration error of the term with
f_{{\hat{\rho}}}
is, up to logarithmic factors and a
\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}
factor,

\displaystyle\E_{\delta^{\prime}\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\sum_{p,q,r,s}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}+\frac{d^{\frac{p+1+q}{2}}}{n}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}\Big)

(C.171)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\E_{\delta^{\prime}\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\sum_{p\leq 3,q\leq 4}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}+\frac{d^{\frac{p+1+q}{2}}}{n}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}\Big)

(C.172)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\E_{\delta^{\prime}\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\sum_{p\leq 3,q\leq 4}\frac{d^{\frac{p+q+1}{2}}}{n}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}

(C.173)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\E_{\delta^{\prime}\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\sum_{p\leq 3,q\leq 4}\frac{d^{\frac{p+q+1}{2}}}{n}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}

(C.174)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\sum_{p\leq 3,q\leq 4}\frac{d^{\frac{p+q+1}{2}}}{n}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\E_{\delta^{\prime}\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}

(C.175)

Finally, in order to simplify this bound, we isolate the dominant terms among the terms of the form
\frac{d^{\frac{p+q+1}{2}}}{n}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{p+1}\E_{\delta^{\prime}\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{q}
, with the following cases. First, consider all the terms where
p=0
. For
q\leq 2
we can bound these terms by
\frac{d^{\frac{p+q+1}{2}}}{n}\overline{\Delta}^{q}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}
, and for
q>2
we can bound these terms by
\frac{d^{\frac{p+q+1}{2}}}{n}\cdot\overline{\Delta}^{2}\cdot B_{2}^{q-2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}
. Thus, out of these terms, the term with
q=4
is dominant, and it contributes

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2.5}}{n}\cdot\overline{\Delta}^{2}\cdot B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}

(C.176)

Next, consider the case where
p\geq 1
. In this case, since the bound
B_{2}
on
\Delta_{\textup{max},t}
is greater than
\frac{1}{d^{1/2}}
, the term with
p=3
and
q=4
dominates, and it contributes

\displaystyle\frac{d^{4}}{n}\cdot B_{2}^{2}\overline{\Delta}^{2}\cdot B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{d^{4}}{n}B_{2}^{4}\overline{\Delta}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.177)

However, we must lastly consider the case where
p\geq 1
and
q<2
— the bound on the concentration error may be larger for
q=1
than
q=2
since
\overline{\Delta}<\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. In this case, since
B_{2}>\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, we must consider the
p=3,q=1
and
p=3,q=0
terms, which respectively contribute

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2.5}}{n}\overline{\Delta}B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.178)

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{n}B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.179)

Note that the
p=3,q=0
term dominates since our bound on
\overline{\Delta}
is less than
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, meaning we can ignore the
p=3,q=1
term. Thus our overall bound on the concentration error originating from the first term in
Equation

C.163
is

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}\cdot\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{2}}{n}B_{2}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{d^{2.5}}{n}\overline{\Delta}^{2}B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{4}}{n}B_{2}^{4}\overline{\Delta}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

(C.180)

To deal with the terms originating from
y(x)
, we observe that we can simply treat
\langle e_{1},x\rangle
in the same way as we deal with
\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle
, and thus the terms originating from
y(x)
do not make any new contributions to the concentration error.

Concentration for Mean of Second Term in
Equation

C.163
.

Finally, to deal with the second term on the right-hand side of
Equation

C.163
, we note that we can follow the same proof, but instead obtaining an upper bound on the concentration of

\displaystyle\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+r=2,4\\
q+s=2,4\end{subarray}}\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}d^{\frac{p+q+r+s}{2}}\langle\delta_{t},x\rangle^{p}\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{q}\langle\bar{u}_{t},x\rangle^{r}\langle\bar{u}_{t}^{\prime},x\rangle^{s}

(C.181)

and as before, the contribution of the terms originating from
y(x)
is at most the contribution of the terms in
Equation

C.181
. Due to the additional
\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
factor, we can in fact ignore the contribution of the terms in
Equation

C.181
, as the dominant terms will be less than the dominant terms from
Equation

C.166
.

Concluding to Bound
C_{t}
.

In summary, with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
, for all choices of
\delta_{t},\delta_{t}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(g)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}\cdot\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{2}}{n}B_{2}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{d^{2.5}}{n}\overline{\Delta}^{2}B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{4}}{n}B_{2}^{4}\overline{\Delta}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

(C.182)

where
g(x)=\langle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}}\ell^{(x)}({\hat{\rho}}_{t}),\delta_{t}\rangle
. Since
C_{t}
is exactly equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i})-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[g(x)]

(C.183)

up to signs and constant factors, we therefore have

\displaystyle|C_{t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{\textup{max}}^{2}(\log d)^{O(1)}\cdot\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{2}}{n}B_{2}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{d^{2.5}}{n}\overline{\Delta}^{2}B_{2}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{d^{4}}{n}B_{2}^{4}\overline{\Delta}^{2}\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\,.

(C.184)

∎

C.4

Additional Lemmas

Lemma C.12

(Uniform Bound on
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
)
.

For any
t\in[-1,1]
, we have
|\sigma(t)|\leq|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}
, and additionally,
|\sigma^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}
. Finally, we have
|\sigma^{\prime\prime}(t)|\lesssim|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.12
.

The first statement is by the definition of
\sigma
, and because
|{P_{2,d}}(t)|,|{P_{4,d}}(t)|\leq 1
for any
t\in[-1,1]
, by
Atkinson and Han 2012
, Eq. (2.116). The second and third statements are by the definition of
\sigma
and using
Equation

A.4
.
∎

Lemma C.13

(Error from Finite Width)
.

Let
\rho
be a distribution on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and let
{\bar{\rho}}
be a distribution on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
of support at most
m
, where the elements of the support of
{\bar{\rho}}
are sampled i.i.d. from
\rho
. Additionally, let
\delta>0
. Then, for some absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(d^{2}+\log\frac{1}{\delta})(\log d)^{C}}{m}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.185)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
f_{\rho}
and
f_{{\bar{\rho}}}
are defined as in
Equation

3.1
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.13
.

We can write
f_{\rho}(x)=\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma(\langle u,x\rangle)]
, and
f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sigma(\langle u_{i},x\rangle)
. Now, let
B\in(0,1)
, which we will choose appropriately later, and define the function
{\varphi_{B}}:[-1,1]\to[-1,1]
by

\displaystyle{\varphi_{B}}(t)=\begin{cases}t&t\in[-B,B]\\
B&t>B\\
-B&t<-B\end{cases}

(C.186)

Additionally, define
\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x)=\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u,x\rangle))]
, and define
\tilde{f}_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{i},x\rangle))
. Let us analyze the error incurred from replacing
f_{\rho}
be
\tilde{f}_{\rho}
and
f_{{\bar{\rho}}}
by
\tilde{f}_{{\bar{\rho}}}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(f_{\rho}(x))^{2}-2\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}f_{\rho}(x)\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x)+\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x))^{2}

(C.187)

We can simplify the second term on the right-hand side by expanding
f_{\rho}
and
\tilde{f}_{\rho}
:

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}f_{\rho}(x)\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\E_{u_{1},u_{2}\sim\rho}\sigma(\langle u_{1},x\rangle)\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{2},x\rangle))

(C.188)

\displaystyle=\E_{u_{1},u_{2}\sim\rho}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sigma(\langle u_{1},x\rangle)\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{2},x\rangle))

(C.189)

To simplify the inner expectation, we use
Proposition

G.5
to find that
{\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{2},x\rangle)=\langle u_{2},x\rangle
with probability at least
1-2\exp(-\frac{B^{2}d}{2})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(f_{\rho}(x))^{2}-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}f_{\rho}(x)\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x)\Big|

(C.190)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\E_{u_{1},u_{2}\sim\rho}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|\sigma(\langle u_{1},x\rangle)|\Big|\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{2},x\rangle))-\sigma(\langle u_{2},x\rangle)\Big|

(C.191)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\E_{u_{2}\sim\rho}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big|\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{2},x\rangle))-\sigma(\langle u_{2},x\rangle)\Big|

(By
Lemma

C.12
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{B^{2}d}{2}\Big)\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})

(By
Proposition

G.5
and
Lemma

C.12
)

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(f_{\rho}(x))^{2}-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x))^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{B^{2}d}{2}\Big)\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})

(C.192)

and therefore,

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{B^{2}d}{2}\Big)\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})

(C.193)

By the same argument, we can show that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x)-\tilde{f}_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\Big(-\frac{B^{2}d}{2}\Big)\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})

(C.194)

Therefore, it suffices to show that
\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x)-\tilde{f}_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]
is small with high probability over
u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}
. Observe that for
|t|\leq B
, we have
|{P_{2,d}}(t)|\leq B^{2}+O(1/d)
, and
|{P_{4,d}}(t)|\leq B^{4}+O(B^{2}/d)+O(1/d^{2})
. Thus, if we choose
B\geq 1/\sqrt{d}
, then for
|t|\leq B
we have
|{P_{2,d}}(t)|\lesssim B^{2}
and
|{P_{4,d}}(t)|\lesssim B^{4}
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle|\sigma(t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}B^{2}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}B^{4}\,.

(C.195)

Now, letting
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and using Hoeffding’s inequality, we find that if
u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}
are i.i.d. samples from
\rho
, then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{1},x\rangle)-\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u,x\rangle))]\Big|\leq t

(C.196)

with probability at least
1-2\exp\Big(-\frac{Cmt^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}B^{4}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}B^{8}}\Big)
for some universal constant
C
. Now, by Corollary 4.2.13 of
Vershynin 2018
, for any
\gamma>0
, there exists
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
of size at most
(\frac{C}{\gamma})^{d}
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists
y\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that
\|x-y\|_{2}\leq\gamma
. Thus, with probability at least
1-2\Big(\frac{C}{\gamma}\Big)^{d}\exp\Big(-\frac{Cmt^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}B^{4}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}B^{8}}\Big)
, for all
y\in\mathcal{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{1},y\rangle)-\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u,y\rangle))]\Big|\leq t\,.

(C.197)

If
Equation

C.197
holds for any
y\in\mathcal{N}
, then for any
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, since
|\sigma^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}
for any
t\in[-1,1]
(see
Lemma

C.12
), if we let
y\in\mathcal{N}
such that
\|x-y\|_{2}\leq\gamma
, then we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u_{1},x\rangle))-\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma({\varphi_{B}}(\langle u,x\rangle))]\Big|\leq t+(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\gamma\,.

(C.198)

In summary, with probability at least
1-2\Big(\frac{C}{\gamma}\Big)^{d}\exp\Big(-\frac{Cmt^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}B^{4}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}B^{8}}\Big)
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\tilde{f}_{\rho}(x)-\tilde{f}_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq t^{2}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\gamma^{2}\,.

(C.199)

Combining
Equation

C.199
,
Equation

C.193
and
Equation

C.194
, we have that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\gamma^{2}

(C.200)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\exp\Big(-\frac{B^{2}d}{2}\Big)\cdot(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})

(C.201)

with probability at least
1-2\Big(\frac{C}{\gamma}\Big)^{d}\exp\Big(-\frac{Cmt^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}B^{4}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}B^{8}}\Big)
. Choosing
