B=\frac{C^{\prime}(\log d)^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}
for a sufficiently large universal constant
C^{\prime}
, and using the fact that
N_{2,d}\lesssim d^{2}
and
N_{4,d}\lesssim d^{4}
(by Eq. (2.10) of
Atkinson and Han 2012
) we have that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}d^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}d^{4})\gamma^{2}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}d^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}d^{4}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.202)

with probability at least
1-2\Big(\frac{C}{\gamma}\Big)^{d}\exp\Big(-\Omega\Big(\frac{mt^{2}}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{16}}\Big)\Big)
. We can rearrange the failure probability to find that, up to constant factors, it is at most
\exp\Big(-\Omega\Big(\frac{mt^{2}}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{16}}\Big)+d\log\frac{C}{\gamma}\Big)
. Finally, choosing
\gamma=\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
, and choosing
t
so that the failure probability is at most
\delta
, we obtain

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{{\bar{\rho}}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{16}(d\log\frac{C}{\gamma}+\log\frac{1}{\delta})}{m}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}d^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}d^{4}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(C.203)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{16}(d^{2}\log d+\log\frac{1}{\delta})}{m}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}

(B.c.
\gamma=\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, as desired.
∎

Lemma C.14

(Bound on Gradient)
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\rho
be a distribution with support on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. In addition, let
\nabla_{u}L(\rho)
and
{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho)
be defined as in
Equation

3.4
. Then, we have
\|\nabla_{u}L(\rho)\|_{2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}
and
\|{\textup{grad}}_{u}L(\rho)\|_{2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}
.

Similarly, let
\nabla_{u}\widehat{L}(\rho)
and
{\textup{grad}}_{u}\widehat{L}(\rho)
be defined as in
Equation

3.5
. Then, we have
\|\nabla_{u}\widehat{L}(\rho)\|_{2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}
and
\|{\textup{grad}}_{u}\widehat{L}(\rho)\|_{2}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.14
.

First, we will bound
\nabla_{u}L(\rho)
for any
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and distribution
\rho
with support in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{u}L(\rho)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(\langle u,x\rangle)x]\Big\|_{2}

(C.204)

\displaystyle\leq\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}|f_{\rho}(x)-y(x)||\sigma^{\prime}(\langle u,x\rangle)|

(C.205)

By
Lemma

C.12
, for any
u,x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have
|\sigma^{\prime}(\langle u,x\rangle)|\lesssim|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}
. Additionally, by
Equation

3.1
and by the definition of
y(x)
, we have
|f_{\rho}(x)-y(x)|\leq|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}
by
Lemma

C.12
. Thus,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{u}L(\rho)\|_{2}\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}

(C.206)

because
N_{2,d}\lesssim d^{2}
and
N_{4,d}\lesssim d^{4}
by Eq. (2.10) of
Atkinson and Han 2012
. By the same argument, for any
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\|(I-uu^{\top})\nabla_{u}L(\rho)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}

(C.207)

because
I-uu^{\top}
is an orthogonal projection matrix. This completes the proof — the proof of the analogous statements for
\nabla_{u}\widehat{L}(\rho)
and
{\textup{grad}}_{u}\widehat{L}(\rho)
follow from the same arguments.
∎

Lemma C.15
.

Let
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\top}x)-\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\prime\top}x))^{2}(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{4}

(C.208)

and

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\top}x)^{2}(u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\,.

(C.209)

Proof of
Lemma

C.15
.

For convenience, let
\delta:=u^{\prime}-u
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\top}x)-\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\prime\top}x))^{2}(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

(C.210)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[({P_{2,d}}^{\prime}(u^{\top}x)-{P_{2,d}}^{\prime}(u^{\prime\top}x))^{2}(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

(C.211)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[({P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(u^{\top}x)-{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}(u^{\prime\top}x))^{2}(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

(By definition of
\sigma
and
\overline{P}_{k,d}
and b.c.
(a+b)^{2}\lesssim a^{2}+b^{2}
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\delta^{\top}x)^{4}]+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[((u^{\top}x)^{3}-(u^{\prime\top}x)^{3})^{2}(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

(C.212)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}}{d^{2}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{4}]

(By
Equation

A.4
and expanding the term with
{P_{4,d}}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\delta^{\top}x)^{4}]+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}\sum_{p+q=2}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(u^{\top}x)^{2p}(u^{\prime\top}x)^{2q}(\delta^{\top}x)^{4}]

(C.213)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}}{d^{2}}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\delta^{\top}x)^{4}]

(C.214)

Here the last inequality is due to the identity
a^{3}-b^{3}=(a-b)(a^{2}+ab+b^{2})
, which gives
((u^{\prime\top}x)^{3}-(u^{\top}x)^{3})^{2}=((u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}+(u^{\prime\top}x)(u^{\top}x)+(u^{\top}x)^{2})^{2}(u^{\prime\top}x-u^{\top}x)^{2}
— expanding the first factor fully gives terms of the form
(u^{\prime\top}x)^{p}(u^{\top}x)^{q}
where
p
and
q
are both even and
p+q=4
. Thus, applying
Lemma

G.17
to the final expression above gives

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\top}x)-\sigma^{\prime}(u^{\prime\top}x))^{2}(u^{\top}x-u^{\prime\top}x)^{2}]

(C.215)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{2}}\|\delta\|_{2}^{4}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}\sum_{p+q=2}\frac{1}{d^{p+q+2}}\|\delta\|_{2}^{4}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d}}{d^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{2}}\|\delta\|_{2}^{4}

(By
Lemma

G.17
)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\lesssim\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}\|\delta\|_{2}^{4}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}\|\delta\|_{2}^{4}

(B.c.
N_{2,d}\lesssim d^{2}
and
N_{4,d}\lesssim d^{4}
(see Equation 2.10 of
Atkinson and Han 2012
))

which completes the proof of the first statement. The proof of the second statement is similar — we expand using the definition of
\sigma
and then apply
Lemma

G.17
to each of the terms. We omit the details of the calculation.
∎

Lemma C.16
.

Let
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
be two distributions on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
which both have support size
m<\infty
, and consider a coupling
(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})
of the two distributions. Additionally, let
f_{\rho}
and
f_{\rho^{\prime}}
be defined as in
Equation

3.1
. Then,

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{\rho^{\prime}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\E_{(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})}\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.216)

Proof of
Lemma

C.16
.

We can write the left-hand side as

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{\rho^{\prime}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\Big(\E_{(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})}[\sigma(\langle u,x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)]\Big)^{2}\Big]

(C.217)

\displaystyle\leq\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\E_{(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})}\Big(\sigma(\langle u,x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)\Big)^{2}

(C.218)

\displaystyle=\E_{(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\sigma(\langle u,x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)\Big)^{2}

(C.219)

For any
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, by the mean-value theorem, there exists
\lambda
which is a convex combination of
\langle u,x\rangle
and
\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle
such that

\displaystyle\sigma(\langle u,x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\prime}(\lambda)(\langle u,x\rangle-\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)\,.

(C.220)

Using a similar argument as in the proof of
Lemma

C.15
, we can show that

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\sigma^{\prime}(\lambda)^{2}(\langle u,x\rangle-\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(C.221)

The proof proceeds by expanding
\sigma^{\prime}(\lambda)
into each of its terms and using the inequality
|a+b|^{3}\lesssim|a|^{3}+|b|^{3}
(which holds for any two real numbers
a,b
) in order to consider only terms which involve one of
u
or
u^{\prime}
— the resulting terms are of the same form as those which appear in
Equation

C.214
. We omit the details of the calculation. Thus,

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-f_{\rho^{\prime}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\E_{(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\sigma(\langle u,x\rangle)-\sigma(\langle u^{\prime},x\rangle)\Big)^{2}

(C.222)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\E_{(u,u^{\prime})\sim(\rho,\rho^{\prime})}\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.223)

as desired.
∎

Appendix D

Proof of
Theorem

3.5

In this section, we build on our analysis from
Appendix

C
to show that projected gradient descent with
\frac{1}{\textup{poly}(d)}
learning rate can achieve low population loss. In the rest of the section, for convenience, we define
{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}=\tilde{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{\eta t}
, and omit
\chi
when it is clear from context.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\rho,\rho^{\prime}
be two distributions on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and let
\Gamma
be a coupling between
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
. Additionally, let
u_{1},u_{2},v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla_{u_{1}}L(\rho)\cdot v-\nabla_{u_{2}}L(\rho^{\prime})\cdot v|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big(\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}\Big[\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}]\Big]^{1/2}+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\Big)\,.

(D.1)

Proof.

We can expand the left-hand side as

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(f_{\rho}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(u_{1}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)-(f_{\rho^{\prime}}(x)-y(x))\sigma^{\prime}(u_{2}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|\,.

(D.2)

We first obtain a bound on

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[f_{\rho}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{1}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)-f_{\rho^{\prime}}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{2}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|\,.

(D.3)

By the definition of
f_{\rho}
, we can rewrite
Equation

D.3
as

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{u_{1}^{\prime},u_{2}^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\sigma(u_{1}^{\prime}\cdot x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{1}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)-\sigma(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{2}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.4)

By
Equation

A.4
,
\sigma
is an even fourth-degree polynomial and
\sigma^{\prime}
is an odd third-degree polynomial. Thus, the inner expectation can be written as a linear combination of a constant number of terms of the form

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(u_{1}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}(u_{1}\cdot x)^{j}(v\cdot x)-(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}(u_{2}\cdot x)^{j}(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.5)

where
i=0,2,4
and
j=1,3
. We can also estimate the coefficient which accompanies each individual term
Equation

D.5
. We first recall the facts that
\sigma(t)=\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}{\bar{P}_{2,d}}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}{\bar{P}_{4,d}}(t)
, that
N_{2,d}=O(d^{2})
and
N_{4,d}=O(d^{4})
by Equation (2.10) of
Atkinson and Han 2012
, and that the
i^{\textup{th}}
degree term of
{P_{2,d}}
has a coefficient which is
O(d^{i/2-1})
and the
i^{\textup{th}}
degree term of
{P_{4,d}}
has a coefficient which is
O(d^{i/2-2})
. From these facts it follows that the
i^{th}
degree term of
{\bar{P}_{2,d}}=\sqrt{N_{2,d}}{P_{2,d}}
has a coefficient which is
O(d^{i/2})
and the
i^{th}
degree term of
{\bar{P}_{4,d}}=\sqrt{N_{4,d}}{P_{4,d}}
has a coefficient which is
O(d^{i/2})
. Thus, each term of the form given in
Equation

D.5
has a coefficient which is at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{(i+j+1)/2}
in absolute value. We can now upper bound each of the terms of the form given in
Equation

D.5
as follows:

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\Big[(u_{1}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}(u_{1}\cdot x)^{j}(v\cdot x)-(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}(u_{2}\cdot x)^{j}(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.6)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\Big((u_{1}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}-(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}\Big)(u_{1}\cdot x)^{j}(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.7)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}\Big((u_{1}\cdot x)^{j}-(u_{2}\cdot x)^{j}\Big)(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.8)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\Big((u_{1}^{\prime}-u_{2}^{\prime})\cdot x\Big)(u_{1}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{k}(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i-1-k}(u_{1}\cdot x)^{j}(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.9)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(u_{2}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i}\Big((u_{1}-u_{2})\cdot x\Big)(u_{1}\cdot x)^{k}(u_{2}\cdot x)^{j-1-k}(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}\|u_{1}^{\prime}-u_{2}^{\prime}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{(1+k+i-1-k+j+1)/2}}+\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{(i+1+k+j-1-k+1)/2}}

(By
Lemma

G.17
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{1}^{\prime}-u_{2}^{\prime}\|_{2}\frac{1}{d^{(i+j+1)/2}}+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\frac{1}{d^{(i+j+1)/2}}\,.

(D.11)

Since the coefficient of this term is at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{(i+j+1)/2}
in absolute value, this term contributes at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big(\|u_{1}^{\prime}-u_{2}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\Big)\,.

(D.12)

Thus, taking the expectation over
u_{1}^{\prime},u_{2}^{\prime}\sim\Gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[f_{\rho}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{1}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)-

\displaystyle f_{\rho^{\prime}}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{2}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.13)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big(\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}\Big[\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}\Big]+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\Big)

(D.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big(\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}\Big[\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\Big]^{1/2}+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\Big)\,.

(D.15)

A similar but simpler argument can be used to bound

\displaystyle\Big|\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[y(x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{1}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)-y(x)\sigma^{\prime}(u_{2}\cdot x)(v\cdot x)\Big]\Big|

(D.16)

by expanding
y(x)
and
\sigma^{\prime}(x)
. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle|\nabla_{u_{1}}L(\rho)\cdot v-\nabla_{u_{2}}L(\rho)\cdot v|

\displaystyle\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big(\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}\Big[\|u-u^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}]\Big]^{1/2}+\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{2}\Big)

(D.17)

as desired.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.5
. Assume that
n\gtrsim d^{3}(\log d)^{C}
for a sufficiently large universal constant
C>0
. Additionally, assume that
\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|({\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}))\cdot{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

(D.18)

Proof.

We can write the left-hand side of
Equation

D.18
as

\displaystyle({\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-

\displaystyle{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t}))\cdot{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}

(D.19)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big((f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x_{i})-(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})\Big)x_{i}\cdot{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\,.

(D.20)

We proceed by first showing using
Lemma

G.7
that we do not incur much error when we replace the empirical average over the
x_{i}
by an expectation over
x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. We can separate the right hand side of
Equation

D.19
into two parts:

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x_{i})-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x_{i})

(D.21)

and

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(y(x_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x_{i})-y(x_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x_{i})\,.

(D.22)

We wish to show that
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are very close to

\displaystyle M_{1}

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\Big(f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)\Big]

(D.23)

and

\displaystyle M_{2}

\displaystyle=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[\Big(y(x_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x_{i})-y(x_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x_{i})\Big]

(D.24)

respectively, with high probability — then, we will obtain bounds on
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
. We will show that
S_{1}
and
M_{1}
are close — the proof that
S_{2}
and
M_{2}
are close is similar.

Expanding into Monomials

Our proof strategy is to expand the following quantity, for
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
:

\displaystyle G(x)

\displaystyle=\Big(f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x)\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.25)

and obtain concentration bounds for each of the terms. In this proof, we let
\Gamma
denote the natural coupling between
{\bar{\rho}},{\hat{\rho}},{\tilde{\rho}}
which is the distribution over triples
(\bar{u}_{\eta t}(\chi),\hat{u}_{\eta t}(\chi),\tilde{u}_{\eta t}(\chi))
, where
\chi
is sampled from
\{\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{m}\}
.
Then, we can write
G(x)
as

\displaystyle G(x)

\displaystyle=\E_{\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime},\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime},\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}\Big[\Big(\sigma(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)-\sigma(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)\Big)\cdot({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)\Big]\,.

(D.26)

For convenience, let us define

\displaystyle g(x)

\displaystyle=\Big(\sigma(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)-\sigma(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)\Big)\cdot({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)\,.

(D.27)

Clearly we have
G(x)=\E_{\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime},\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime},\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\sim\Gamma}[g(x)]
. Now, we consider the effect of expanding
g(x)
by expanding
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
into monomials that involve
(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)
,
(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)
,
(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)
and
(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)
. Since
\sigma
is an even polynomial of degree
4
and
\sigma^{\prime}
is an odd polynomial of degree
3
, we can write
g(x)
as a linear combination of terms of the following form:

\displaystyle\Big((\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}-(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.28)

where
i_{1}=0,2,4
and
i_{2}=1,3
— each such term also has a constant factor of
O(\hat{\sigma}_{i_{1},d}\hat{\sigma}_{i_{2},d}\sqrt{N_{i_{1},d}}\sqrt{N_{i_{2},d}})
. We now consider the contribution of
Equation

D.28
for each value of
i_{1},i_{2}
. In the case where
i_{1}=0
, we can rewrite
Equation

D.28
as
({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2}
when
i_{2}=1
, and

\displaystyle\Big((\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{2}+(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)+(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{2}\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2}

(D.29)

when
i_{2}=3
. In the case where
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
are both nonzero, letting
{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}=\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}-\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
we can write
Equation

D.28
as

\displaystyle\Big((\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}-(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}

\displaystyle(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.30)

\displaystyle=\Big((\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}-(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}\Big)(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.31)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}\Big((\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}-(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}\Big)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.32)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}+i_{4}=i_{1}-1\\
i_{3},i_{4}\geq 0\end{subarray}}(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x-\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.33)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}+i_{4}=i_{2}-1\\
i_{3},i_{4}\geq 0\end{subarray}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x-\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)

(D.34)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}+i_{4}=i_{1}-1\\
i_{3},i_{4}\geq 0\end{subarray}}(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)

(D.35)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{3}+i_{4}=i_{2}-1\\
i_{3},i_{4}\geq 0\end{subarray}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2}\,.

(D.36)

In summary,
Equation

D.28
can be written as a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)

(D.37)

where
i_{3}+i_{4}=1,3
and
i_{2}=1,3
, and terms of the form

\displaystyle(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2}

(D.38)

where
i_{1}=0,2,4
and
i_{3}+i_{4}=0,2
.

Contribution of
Equation

D.38
to Concentration Error

In
Equation

D.38
, we can write
\tilde{u}_{t}=\hat{u}_{\eta t}+{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}
, and thus write
Equation

D.38
as a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2+i_{3}}

(D.39)

where
i_{1}=0,2,4
and
i_{2}+i_{3}=0,2
. Next, in order to use
Lemma

G.7
, we write
\hat{u}_{\eta t}=\bar{u}_{\eta t}+\delta_{t}
and expand (recalling the definition of
\delta_{t}
from
Lemma

5.4
). Thus,
Equation

D.38
is a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle(\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\delta_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2+i_{5}}

(D.40)

where
i_{1}+i_{2}=0,2,4
, and
i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5}=0,2
. Finally, we obtain a concentration bound for
Equation

D.40
using
Lemma

G.7
:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle(\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{1}}(\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{2}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{3}}(\delta_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{4}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x_{i})^{2+i_{5}}

(D.41)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[(\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{1}}(\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\delta_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{2+i_{5}}\Big]\Big|

(D.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log d)^{O(1)}\frac{\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2+i_{5}}}{d^{(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5})/2+1}}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{(i_{2}+i_{4}+i_{5})/2+1}}{n}+\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}\Big)\,.

(D.43)

Here, when applying
Lemma

G.7
,
\bar{u}_{\eta t}
and
\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
take the role of
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
, while
\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}/\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}
,
\delta_{\eta t}/\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}
and
{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}/\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}
take the role of
u_{1}
,
u_{2}
,
u_{3}
and
u_{4}
(we let
u_{3}=u_{4}={\tilde{\delta}}_{t}/\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}
and choose
p_{3}
and
p_{4}
so that
p_{3}+p_{4}=2+i_{5}
). Note that we implicitly perform a union bound over
\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
and
\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}
drawn from the support of
f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}
— this does not affect the failure probability, since the failure probability for the result of
Lemma

G.7
is
\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}
, and it is assumed in
Theorem

3.5
that
m\leq d^{C}
for some universal constant
C
. When applying
Lemma

G.7
at later points in this proof, we implicitly perform this union bound.

In order to compute the final contribution of each such term to the concentration error between
S_{1}
and
M_{1}
, we must first (1) incorporate the coefficients of
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
with respect to the Legendre polynomials
{P_{2,d}}
and
{P_{4,d}}
, (2) take the average of
\|\delta^{\prime}_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{b}
with respect to
\chi
(which is implicit throughout this proof), and (3) use the fact that
n\gtrsim d^{3}(\log d)^{O(1)}
.

Recall that
\sigma=\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}\sqrt{N_{2,d}}{P_{2,d}}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\sqrt{N_{4,d}}{P_{4,d}}
. Using
Equation

A.4
and the fact that
N_{2,d}\asymp d^{2}
and
N_{4,d}\asymp d^{4}
(by Equation (2.10) of
Atkinson and Han 2012
), we find that, up to constant factors, the coefficient of the
0^{\textup{th}}
-order term in
\sigma(t)
can be bounded above by
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d})
, the coefficient of the
2^{\textup{nd}}
-order term in
\sigma(t)
can be bounded above by
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d})d
, and the coefficient of the
4^{\textup{th}}
order term can be bounded above by
\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}d^{2}
. Similarly, up to constant factors, the coefficient of the
1^{\textup{st}}
-order term in
\sigma^{\prime}(t)
can be bounded above by
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d})d
and the coefficient of the
3^{\textup{rd}}
-order term in
\sigma^{\prime}(t)
can be bounded above by
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d})d^{2}
. Thus, each term of degree
k
originating from
\sigma
contributes a factor of
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d})d^{k/2}
to the coefficient of
Equation

D.38
, and each term of degree
k-1
originating from
\sigma^{\prime}
contributes a factor of
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d})d^{k/2}
to the coefficient of
Equation

D.38
. Finally, for each term in
Equation

D.40
of total degree
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5}+2
, note that
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
together contribute a factor of degree
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5}+1
, since in
Equation

D.27
, aside from the factors contributed by
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
, only a single factor of
{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x
is present.

Thus, to obtain the final contribution of
Equation

D.41
to the overall error between
M_{1}
and
S_{1}
, we multiply by an additional coefficient of
\max(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2},\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5})/2+1}
. Taking this into account, the contribution of
Equation

D.41
is

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2+i_{5}}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{(i_{2}+i_{4}+i_{5})/2+1}}{n}+\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}\Big)\,.

(D.44)

Next, we simplify this using casework on
i_{2}
. For the case
i_{2}\leq 1
, since
i_{4}+i_{5}\leq 2
, the bound is at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2+i_{5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(D.45)

since
d^{(i_{2}+i_{4}+i_{5})/2+1}\leq d^{2.5}
, and because
n\gtrsim d^{3}(\log d)^{O(1)}
, and we eliminate a factor of
\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{i_{5}}
because all of the particles have
\ell_{2}
norm at most
1
. Additionally, for the case
i_{2}\geq 2
, after taking the average of
\|\delta^{\prime}_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{2}}\lesssim\|\delta^{\prime}_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{2}
across all
\chi
, the bound is at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})

\displaystyle(\log d)^{O(1)}\overline{\Delta}_{\eta t}^{2}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{(i_{2}+i_{4}+i_{5})/2+1}}{n}+\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}\Big)

(D.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\overline{\Delta}_{\eta t}^{2}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\Big(d^{i_{2}/2-1}+O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Big)

(B.c.
n\gtrsim d^{3}(\log d)^{O(1)}
and
i_{4}+i_{5}\leq 2
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

(B.c.
\overline{\Delta}_{\eta t}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
and
i_{2}\leq 4
)

where we have
\overline{\Delta}_{\eta t}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
because by
Lemma

C.3
, we have that
5.3
holds as long as
\eta t\leq T_{*,\epsilon}
. In summary, we have shown that terms of the form
Equation

D.38
contribute at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
, up to constant factors, to the concentration error between
S_{1}
and
M_{2}
.

Contribution of
Equation

D.37
to Concentration Error

We expand
Equation

D.37
by using
\tilde{u}_{t}={\tilde{\delta}}_{t}+\delta_{\eta t}+\bar{u}_{\eta t}
,
\hat{u}_{\eta t}=\delta_{\eta t}+\bar{u}_{\eta t}
and
\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}={\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}+\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}+\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
. Thus, we can write
Equation

D.37
as a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{1+i_{1}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{1+i_{2}}(\delta_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{5}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{6}}

(D.47)

where
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5}+i_{6}=6
(since the degrees of each of the monomials must be
8
) and
i_{3}\leq 3
(since each of these terms originates from
(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\tilde{u}_{t}\cdot x)^{i_{2}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)
where
i_{2}\leq 3
). Using
Lemma

G.7
, we obtain a concentration bound for
Equation

D.47
:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x_{i})^{1+i_{1}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x_{i})^{1+i_{2}}(\delta_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{3}}(\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{4}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{5}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x_{i})^{i_{6}}

(D.48)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big[({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot x)^{1+i_{1}}({\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{1+i_{2}}(\delta_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{3}}(\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{4}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}\cdot x)^{i_{5}}(\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x)^{i_{6}}\Big]\Big|

(D.49)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log d)^{O(1)}\frac{\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{1+i_{1}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{1+i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{3}}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}}{d^{1+(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}+i_{5}+i_{6})/2}}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{1+(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4})/2}}{n}+\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}\Big)\,.

(D.50)

As discussed in the previous case, after we consider the coefficients of
\sigma(t)
and
\sigma^{\prime}(t)
, we find that the term in
Equation

D.47
contributes

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(\log d)^{O(1)}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{1+i_{1}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{1+i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{3}}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{1+(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4})/2}}{n}+\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}\Big)\,.

(D.51)

Now, suppose
i_{5}+i_{6}\geq 2
. Then,
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}\leq 4
, meaning the contribution to the concentration error is at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

(D.52)

using the fact that
n\gtrsim d^{3}(\log d)^{C}
for a sufficiently large constant
C
, and
\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
and
\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
. On the other hand, if
i_{5}+i_{6}\leq 1
, then
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}\geq 5
, and since
i_{3}\leq 3
, this implies that
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{4}\geq 2
. In this case, the bound is at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})

\displaystyle(\log d)^{O(1)}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{1+i_{1}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{1+i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{3}}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\frac{d^{1+(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4})/2}}{n}+\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(\log d)}}\Big)

(D.53)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{1+i_{1}}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{1+i_{2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{i_{3}}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}d

(B.c.
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}\leq 6
and
n\gtrsim d^{3}(\log d)^{C}
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{(i_{1}+i_{2})/2}}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{i_{4}}d\,.

(B.c.
\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
and
\|\delta_{\eta t}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
)

Thus, if
i_{1}+i_{2}\geq 2
, then the contribution of this term to the overall concentration error is at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}
. On the other hand, if
i_{4}=1
, then we can take the average of
\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}
across
\chi
, and by
Lemma

C.3
, we have that
5.3
holds for
\eta t\leq T_{*,\epsilon}
, meaning that
\E_{\chi}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq\overline{\Delta}_{\eta t}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. In this case, the contribution of this term to the overall concentration error is at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{(i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{4})/2}}d=(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}
. Finally, when
i_{4}\geq 2
, we can take the average of
\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}
over
\chi
— we have
\E_{\chi}\|\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{d}
, and therefore the contribution of this term to the overall concentration error is at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{1+(i_{1}+i_{2})/2}}d\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(D.54)

In all cases, the contribution of
Equation

D.37
to the overall concentration error between
S_{1}
and
M_{1}
is at most

\displaystyle(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(D.55)

Overall Concentration Error

In summary, the overall concentration error between
S_{1}
and
M_{1}
is that of
G(x)
. We have expanded
G(x)
into various monomials of dot products involving
x
, and shown that each monomial contributes at most
(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}
to the concentration error. Thus, with high probability,

\displaystyle|M_{1}-S_{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(D.56)

In order to bound
|M_{2}-S_{2}|
, an almost identical, but simpler, argument can be used, with
y(x)
in the place of
f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x)
or
f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x)
. In the modified argument,
\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}
,
\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
and
\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
will all be replaced with
e_{1}
. Thus, in the analysis of
Equation

D.40
, we can replace
\bar{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
with
e_{1}
and assume that
i_{2}=0
(since otherwise this term becomes
0
due to the
\delta_{\eta t}^{\prime}\cdot x
factor) — we can then proceed similarly to the subcase where
i_{2}\leq 1
in the analysis of
Equation

D.40
. Additionally, the term analogous to
Equation

D.47
does not arise in the case where
f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x)
and
f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x)
are replaced by
y(x)
, since
\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}
and
\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}
are both replaced by
e_{1}
, so the corresponding term is
0
. We also note that the Legendre coefficients of
y(x)
have absolute values which are less than those of
\sigma
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle|M_{1}-S_{1}|+|M_{2}-S_{2}|\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(D.57)

Bounding the Expectation

To complete the proof, it suffices to bound
|M_{1}-M_{2}|
. We have that

\displaystyle M_{1}-M_{2}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tilde{u}_{t}}L({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})\cdot{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}-\nabla_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}L({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t})\cdot{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}

(D.58)

and by
Lemma

D.1
, we have

\displaystyle|M_{1}-M_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big(\E_{(\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime},\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime})\sim({\tilde{\rho}}_{t},{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t})}\Big[\|\tilde{u}_{t}^{\prime}-\hat{u}_{\eta t}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\Big]^{1/2}+\|\tilde{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{\eta t}\|_{2}\Big)\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}

(D.59)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

(D.60)

Combining this with our bound on
|M_{1}-S_{1}|+|M_{2}-S_{2}|
, we find that

\displaystyle|({\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}))\cdot{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

(D.61)

as desired.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Suppose we are in the setting of
Theorem

3.5
. Suppose
s,t\geq 0
. Then,

\displaystyle\|{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})\|_{2}\leq(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.62)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\|{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})

\displaystyle-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})\|_{2}

(D.63)

\displaystyle=\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})\nabla_{\hat{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})\nabla_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})\|_{2}

(D.64)

\displaystyle=\Big\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})x_{i}

(D.65)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})x_{i}\Big\|_{2}

(D.66)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}-(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}\,.

(D.67)

We bound the right-hand side by several applications of the triangle inequality. First, observe that for any
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\sigma(\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})\cdot x)-\sigma(\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\cdot x)|

(D.68)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})\cdot x-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\cdot x|

(By
Lemma

C.12
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.69)

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}\,.

(D.70)

Additionally, for
\hat{u}_{t}
and
\hat{u}_{s}
with the same initialization
\chi
, we have

\displaystyle|\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x)-\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\cdot|\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x-\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x|

(By
Lemma

C.12
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\|\hat{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{s}\|_{2}\,.

(D.71)

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}\,.

(D.72)

Also, for
\hat{u}_{t}
and
\hat{u}_{s}
with the same initialization
\chi
, we have

\displaystyle\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}-(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(\hat{u}_{t}\cdot x_{i})\hat{u}_{t}-(\hat{u}_{s}\cdot x_{i})\hat{u}_{s}\|_{2}

(D.73)

\displaystyle\leq\|(\hat{u}_{t}\cdot x_{i}-\hat{u}_{s}\cdot x_{i})\hat{u}_{t}\|_{2}+|\hat{u}_{s}\cdot x_{i}|\|\hat{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{s}\|_{2}

(D.74)

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}\,.

(D.75)

Finally, for
x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and any distribution
\rho
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{\rho}(x)-y(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}|\sqrt{N_{2,d}}+|\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}|\sqrt{N_{4,d}}

(D.76)

since we can bound
|f_{\rho}(x)|
using
Lemma

C.12
, and because (ignoring
\hat{h}_{0,d}
since it is equal to
\hat{\sigma}_{0,d}
)
y(x)=\gamma_{2}\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}{P_{2,d}}+\gamma_{4}\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}{P_{4,d}}
, and using
3.2
.

Thus, we can simplify the right-hand side of
Equation

D.67
using the triangle inequality, and combining
Equation

D.70
,
Equation

D.72
,
Equation

D.75
and
Equation

D.76
:

\displaystyle\|(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}-(f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i}))\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(D.77)

\displaystyle\leq|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{t}}(x_{i})-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})|\|\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(D.78)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i})||\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})-\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})|\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(D.79)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i})||\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})|\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}-(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(D.80)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.81)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i})||\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})-\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})|\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(D.82)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i})||\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})|\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}-(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(By
Equation

D.70
, bounding
|\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{t}^{\top}x_{i})|
by
Lemma

C.12
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.83)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.84)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+|f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{s}}(x_{i})-y(x_{i})||\sigma^{\prime}(\hat{u}_{s}^{\top}x_{i})|\|(I-\hat{u}_{t}\hat{u}_{t}^{\top})x_{i}-(I-\hat{u}_{s}\hat{u}_{s}^{\top})x_{i}\|_{2}

(By
Equation

D.76
and
Equation

D.72
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.85)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.86)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(By
Equation

D.75
,
Equation

D.76
and
Lemma

C.12
)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}\,.

(D.87)

Therefore,
Equation

D.67
gives

\displaystyle\|{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}N_{2,d}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}N_{4,d})\cdot\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{t}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(D.88)

as desired.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

3.5
.

To show that projected gradient descent achieves low population loss, we show that it does not diverge far from projected gradient descent. Suppose
{\hat{\rho}}_{0}
and
{\tilde{\rho}}_{0}
are both equal to
\textup{unif}(\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{m}\})
. Then, by induction over
t
, we bound the difference between
\tilde{u}_{t}(\chi)
and
\hat{u}_{\eta t}(\chi)
for all
\chi\in\{\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{m}\}
. At
t=0
, we have
\tilde{u}_{0}(\chi)=\hat{u}_{0}(\chi)
. Let
t
be a nonnegative integer, and assume that for all
s\leq t
we have
\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
.

By the definition of projected gradient descent, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{t+1}(\chi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{u}_{t}(\chi)-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}(\chi)}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})}{\|\tilde{u}_{t}(\chi)-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}(\chi)}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})\|_{2}}\,.

(D.89)

Note that
\|\tilde{u}_{t}-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})\|_{2}\geq\|\tilde{u}_{t}\|_{2}
, since
{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})
is orthogonal to
\tilde{u}_{t}
. Thus, letting
\Pi
denote the projection onto the
\ell_{2}
unit ball, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}_{t+1}-\hat{u}_{\eta(t+1)}\|_{2}=\|\Pi(\tilde{u}_{t}-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t}))-\Pi(\hat{u}_{\eta(t+1)})\|_{2}\leq\|\tilde{u}_{t}-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-\hat{u}_{\eta(t+1)}\|_{2}\,.

(D.90)

Also, we have

\displaystyle\hat{u}_{\eta(t+1)}(\chi)

\displaystyle=\hat{u}_{\eta t}(\chi)-\int_{\eta t}^{\eta(t+1)}{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}(\chi)}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})ds\,.

(D.91)

Thus,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}_{t}-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-\hat{u}_{\eta(t+1)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\tilde{u}_{t}-\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-\hat{u}_{\eta t}+\int_{\eta t}^{\eta(t+1)}{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})ds\Big\|_{2}^{2}

(D.92)

\displaystyle=\|\tilde{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{\eta t}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big\|\eta\cdot{\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-\int_{\eta t}^{\eta(t+1)}{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})ds\Big\|_{2}^{2}

(D.93)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2(\tilde{u}_{t}-\hat{u}_{\eta t})\cdot\int_{\eta t}^{\eta(t+1)}({\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s}))ds

(D.94)

\displaystyle=\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)

(D.95)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\eta{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot({\textup{grad}}_{\tilde{u}_{t}}\widehat{L}({\tilde{\rho}}_{t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}))

(D.96)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot\int_{\eta t}^{\eta(t+1)}({\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})ds

(By
Lemma

C.14
)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)+O\Big(\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

(D.97)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\cdot\int_{\eta t}^{\eta(t+1)}({\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})ds

(By
Lemma

D.2
)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)+O\Big(\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

(D.98)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\eta\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}\max_{s\in[\eta t,\eta(t+1)]}\|{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{\eta t}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t})-{\textup{grad}}_{\hat{u}_{s}}\widehat{L}({\hat{\rho}}_{s})\|_{2}

(D.99)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)+O\Big(\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

(D.100)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\eta\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}\max_{s\in[\eta t,\eta(t+1)]}\max_{i\in[m]}\|\hat{u}_{s}(\chi_{i})-\hat{u}_{\eta t}(\chi_{i})\|_{2}

(By
Lemma

D.3
)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)+O\Big(\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

(D.101)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\eta\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}\cdot\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{4}

(By
Lemma

C.14
)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)+O\Big(\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\,.

(D.102)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t+1}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big)\Big)\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}+O\Big(\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\Big)

(D.103)

which means that for some universal constant
C
,

\displaystyle\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t+1}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{t}\Big(1+C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big)^{i}\cdot\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}

(D.104)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\cdot\frac{\Big(1+C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big)^{t+1}-1}{\Big(1+C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big)-1}

(D.105)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})^{2}d^{8}\cdot\frac{\Big(1+C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big)^{t+1}-1}{C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})}

(D.106)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{8}\cdot\Big(1+C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\Big)^{t+1}

(D.107)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{8}\cdot e^{C\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})(t+1)}\,.

(D.108)

Thus, as long as
\eta=\frac{1}{(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{B}}
for a sufficiently large universal constant
B
, and choosing
\eta
so that
T_{*,\epsilon}
is an integer multiple of
\eta
, we have
\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t+1}(\chi)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, because if
t\leq\frac{T_{*,\epsilon}}{\eta}
, then

\displaystyle\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t+1}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{8}\cdot e^{C(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})T_{*,\epsilon}}

(D.109)

\displaystyle\leq\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{8}\cdot e^{O(\log d)}

(D.110)

\displaystyle\leq\eta(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})d^{O(1)}\,.

(D.111)

This completes the induction, and therefore for all
t\leq\frac{T_{*,\epsilon}}{\eta}
, we have
\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
. Additionally, by
Lemma

C.16
, and using the coupling between
{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}
and
{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}
which is the joint distribution of
(\tilde{u}_{t}(\chi),\hat{u}_{\eta t}(\chi))
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{{\tilde{\rho}}_{t}}(x)-f_{{\hat{\rho}}_{\eta t}}(x))^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\max_{\chi}\|{\tilde{\delta}}_{t}(\chi)\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{d}\lesssim(\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}+\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2})\epsilon

(D.112)

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix E

Missing Proofs in
Section

3

E.1

Proof of
Lemma

3.1

In this section, we prove sufficient and necessary conditions for
\gamma_{2}
and
\gamma_{4}
when the population loss is 0.

Proposition E.1

(Necessary condition)
.

Let
f_{\rho}(x)=\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma(u^{\top}x)]
be a (not necessarily symmetric nor rotational invariant) two-layer neural network and
y(x)=h(e_{1}^{\top}x)
the target function. Define
\gamma_{k}=\frac{\hat{h}_{k,d}}{\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}},\forall k\geq 0
. Suppose
\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(f_{\rho}(x)-y(x))^{2}=0
and
\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}\neq 0,\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{2}\leq\gamma_{4}+O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right),

(E.1)

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}\leq\gamma_{2}+O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right),

(E.2)

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}\leq 1.

(E.3)

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-h(x^{\top}e_{1}))^{2}]=\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma(u^{\top}x)]-h(x^{\top}e_{1})\Big)^{2}

(E.4)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\E_{u\sim\rho}\Big[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}\overline{P_{k,d}}(u^{\top}x)\Big]-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{h}_{k,d}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}e_{1})\Big)^{2}

(E.5)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}\E_{u\sim\rho}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(u^{\top}x)]-\hat{h}_{k,d}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}e_{1})\Big)\Big)^{2}.

(E.6)

Continuing the equation by invoking Lemma
A.5
, we get

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}\E_{u\sim\rho}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(u^{\top}x)]-\hat{h}_{k,d}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}e_{1})\Big)\Big)^{2}

(E.7)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\Big(\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}\E_{u\sim\rho}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(u^{\top}x)]-\hat{h}_{k,d}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}e_{1})\Big)^{2}

(E.8)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}^{2}\Big(\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[P_{k,d}(u^{\top}u^{\prime})-2\gamma_{k}P_{k,d}(u^{\top}e_{1})+\gamma_{k}^{2}P_{k,d}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1}])\Big)^{2}.

(E.9)

Therefore,
\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[(f_{\rho}(x)-h(x^{\top}e_{1}))^{2}]=0
implies that

\displaystyle\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[P_{2,d}(u^{\top}u^{\prime})-2\gamma_{2}P_{2,d}(u^{\top}e_{1})+\gamma_{2}^{2}P_{2,d}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})]=0,

(E.10)

\displaystyle\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[P_{4,d}(u^{\top}u^{\prime})-2\gamma_{4}P_{4,d}(u^{\top}e_{1})+\gamma_{4}^{2}P_{4,d}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})]=0.

(E.11)

First we prove Eq. (
E.3
). By Lemma
A.6
, there is a feature mapping
\phi:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{2,d}}
such that for every
u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\left<\phi(u),\phi(v)\right>=N_{2,d}P_{2,d}(u^{\top}v),

(E.12)

\displaystyle\|\phi_{u}\|_{2}^{2}=N_{2,d}.

(E.13)

Rewrite Eq. (
E.10
) using
\phi_{2}
we get

\displaystyle\|\E_{u\sim\rho}[\phi_{2}(u)]-\gamma_{2}\phi_{2}(e_{1})\|_{2}^{2}=0.

(E.14)

Or equivalently,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}\phi_{2}(e_{1})=\E_{u\sim\rho}[\phi_{2}(u)]

(E.15)

As a result,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}=\left\lVert\frac{\phi_{2}(e_{1})}{\sqrt{N_{k,d}}}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\frac{\E_{u\sim\rho}[\phi_{2}(u)]}{\sqrt{N_{k,d}}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\E_{u\sim\rho}[\left\lVert\phi_{2}(u)]\right\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{N_{2,d}}}=1.

(E.16)

Similarly, we also have
\gamma_{4}\leq 1.

Now we prove Eq. (
E.1
). Recall that

\displaystyle P_{2,d}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d-1}t^{2}-\frac{1}{d-1},

(E.17)

\displaystyle P_{4,d}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{(d+2)(d+4)}{d^{2}-1}t^{4}-\frac{6d+12}{d^{2}-1}t^{2}+\frac{3}{d^{2}-1}.

(E.18)

Combining Eq. (
E.10
) and Eq. (
E.17
) we have

\displaystyle 0=\frac{d-1}{d}\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[P_{2,d}(u^{\top}u^{\prime})-2\gamma_{2}P_{2,d}(u^{\top}e_{1})+\gamma_{2}^{2}P_{2,d}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})]

(E.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}u^{\prime})^{2}-2\gamma_{2}(u^{\top}e_{1})^{2}+\gamma_{2}^{2}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})^{2}]-\frac{1}{d}(1-2\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{2}^{2})

(E.20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\|\E_{u\sim\rho}[u^{\otimes 2}-\gamma_{2}e_{1}^{\otimes 2}]\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{1}{d}(1-\gamma_{2})^{2}.

(E.21)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\E_{u\sim\rho}[u^{\otimes 2}-\gamma_{2}e_{1}^{\otimes 2}]\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{d}(1-\gamma_{2})^{2}.

(E.22)

Similarly, combining Eq. (
E.11
) and Eq. (
E.18
) we get

\displaystyle 0=\frac{d^{2}-1}{(d+2)(d+4)}\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[P_{4,d}(u^{\top}u^{\prime})-4\gamma_{4}P_{4,d}(u^{\top}e_{1})+\gamma_{4}^{2}P_{4,d}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})]

(E.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}u^{\prime})^{4}-2\gamma_{4}(u^{\top}e_{1})^{4}+\gamma_{4}^{2}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})^{4}]+\frac{3}{(d+2)(d+4)}(1-2\gamma_{4}+\gamma_{4}^{2})

(E.24)

\displaystyle-\frac{6d+12}{(d+2)(d+4)}\E_{u,u^{\prime}\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}u^{\prime})^{2}-2\gamma_{4}(u^{\top}e_{1})^{2}+\gamma_{4}^{2}(e_{1}^{\top}e_{1})^{2}].

(E.25)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\E_{u\sim\rho}[u^{\otimes 4}-\gamma_{4}e_{1}^{\otimes 4}]\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{3}{(d+2)(d+4)}(1-\gamma_{4})^{2}+\frac{6d+12}{(d+2)(d+4)}(\gamma_{4}+1)^{2}.

(E.26)

Now for any fixed
\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, our key observation is that by Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{2}]^{2}\leq\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{4}].

(E.27)

On the one hand, by Eq. (
E.22
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{2}]-\gamma_{2}(e_{1}^{\top}\xi)^{2}\right|=\left|\E_{u\sim\rho}[\left<u^{\otimes 2}-\gamma_{2}e_{1}^{\otimes 2},\xi^{\otimes 2}\right>]\right|

(E.28)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\|\E_{u\sim\rho}[u^{\otimes 2}-\gamma_{2}e_{1}^{\otimes 2}]\|_{F}\|\xi^{\otimes 2}\|_{F}=\sqrt{\frac{(1-\gamma_{2})^{2}}{d}}.

(E.29)

On the other hand, by Eq. (
E.26
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{4}]-\gamma_{4}(e_{1}^{\top}\xi)^{4}\right|=\left|\E_{u\sim\rho}[\left<u^{\otimes 4}-\gamma_{4}e_{1}^{\otimes 4},\xi^{\otimes 4}\right>]\right|

(E.30)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\|\E_{u\sim\rho}[u^{\otimes 4}-\gamma_{4}e_{1}^{\otimes 4}]\|_{F}\|\xi^{\otimes 4}\|_{F}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{6d+12}{(d+2)(d+4)}}(|\gamma_{4}+1|+|\gamma_{4}-1|).

(E.31)

Finally, combining Eqs (
E.27
)-(
E.31
) and set
\xi=e_{1}
we get

\displaystyle\left(\gamma_{2}-\sqrt{\frac{(1-\gamma_{2})^{2}}{d}}\right)^{2}\leq\gamma_{4}+2\sqrt{\frac{6d+12}{(d+2)(d+4)}}(|\gamma_{4}+1|+|\gamma_{4}-1|).

(E.32)

It follows directly that

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{2}\leq\gamma_{4}+O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right).

(E.33)

Finally we prove Eq. (
E.2
). For any
\xi,u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have
|u^{\top}\xi|\leq 1
. As a result,

\displaystyle\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{4}]\leq\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{2}].

(E.34)

Combining with Eq. (
E.29
) and Eq. (
E.31
) and setting
\xi=e_{1}
we get

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}\leq O(1/\sqrt{d})+\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{4}]\leq O(1/\sqrt{d})+\E_{u\sim\rho}[(u^{\top}\xi)^{2}]\leq O(1/\sqrt{d})+\gamma_{2}.

(E.35)

∎

Proposition E.2

(Sufficient condition)
.

Let
f_{\rho}(x)=\E_{u\sim\rho}[\sigma(u^{\top}x)]
be a two-layer neural network and
y(x)=h(e_{1}^{\top}x)
the target function. Let
\gamma_{k}=\frac{\hat{h}_{k,d}}{\hat{\sigma}_{k,d}},\forall k\geq 0
. Suppose
\hat{h}_{k,d}=0,\forall k\not\in\{0,2,4\}
,
\hat{h}_{0,d}=\hat{\sigma}_{0,d}
,
\sigma
is a degree-4 polynomial, and

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{2}\leq\gamma_{4}-O\left(1/d\right),

(E.36)

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}\leq\gamma_{2}-O\left(1/d\right),

(E.37)

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}\leq 1-O\left(1/d\right).

(E.38)

Then there exists a symmetric and rotational invariant neural network
\rho
such that
\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(f_{\rho}(x)-y(x))^{2}=0
.

Proof.

Recall that by Lemma
4.2
, for every symmetric and rotational invariant neural network
\rho
we have

\displaystyle L(\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,d}^{2}}{2}\Big(\E_{u\sim\rho}[P_{2,d}(w)]-\gamma_{2}\Big)^{2}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{4,d}^{2}}{2}\Big(\E_{u\sim\rho}[P_{4,d}(w)]-\gamma_{4}\Big)^{2}

(E.39)

where
u=(w,z)
,
w\in[-1,1]
, and
z\in\sqrt{1-w^{2}}\mathbb{S}^{d-2}.
Therefore, we only need to show that there exists a one-dimensional distribution
\mu
such that

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu}[P_{2,d}(w)]=\gamma_{2},

(E.40)

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu}[P_{4,d}(w)]=\gamma_{4}.

(E.41)

Recall that

\displaystyle P_{2,d}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d-1}t^{2}-\frac{1}{d-1},

(E.42)

\displaystyle P_{4,d}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{(d+2)(d+4)}{d^{2}-1}t^{4}-\frac{6d+12}{d^{2}-1}t^{2}+\frac{3}{d^{2}-1}.

(E.43)

Consequently, Eq. (
E.40
) and Eq. (
E.41
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu}[w^{2}]=\frac{d-1}{d}\gamma_{2}+\frac{1}{d},

(E.44)

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu}[w^{4}]=\frac{d^{2}-1}{(d+2)(d+4)}\gamma_{4}+\frac{6d+12}{(d+2)(d+4)}\left(\frac{d-1}{d}\gamma_{2}+\frac{1}{d}\right)-\frac{3}{(d+2)(d+4)}.

(E.45)

Let
\beta_{2}=\frac{d-1}{d}\gamma_{2}+\frac{1}{d}
and
\beta_{4}=\frac{d^{2}-1}{(d+2)(d+4)}\gamma_{4}+\frac{6d+12}{(d+2)(d+4)}\left(\frac{d-1}{d}\gamma_{2}+\frac{1}{d}\right)-\frac{3}{(d+2)(d+4)}
. Then we have
|\beta_{2}-\gamma_{2}|\leq O(1/d)
and
|\beta_{4}-\gamma_{4}|\leq O(1/d).
Rewriting Eqs. (
E.36
)-(
E.38
) using
\beta_{2}
and
\beta_{4}
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{2}^{2}\leq\beta_{4},

(E.46)

\displaystyle\beta_{4}\leq\beta_{2},

(E.47)

\displaystyle\beta_{2}\leq 1.

(E.48)

By
Proposition

G.14
, there exists a distribution
\mu^{\star}
such that

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu^{\star}}[w^{2}]=\beta_{2},

(E.49)

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu^{\star}}[w^{4}]=\beta_{4}.

(E.50)

Hence, by setting the marginal distribution of
w
equal to
\mu^{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{w\sim\mu^{\star}}[P_{2,d}(w)]=\gamma_{2},\quad\E_{w\sim\mu^{\star}}[P_{4,d}(w)]=\gamma_{4},

(E.51)

which proves the desired result.
∎

Appendix F

Proofs for Sample Complexity Lower Bounds for Kernel Method

In this section, we present a sample complexity lower bound for kernel methods with any inner product kernel. That is, the kernel
K:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
can be written as
K(x,z)=\kappa(x^{\top}z)
for some one-dimensional function
\kappa:[-1,1]\to\mathbb{R}
. Inner product kernels are rotational invariant and do not specialize to any coordinate. In particular, NTK of two-layer neural networks is an inner product kernel (e.g.,
Du et al. 2018
,
Wei et al. 2019b
).

To prove
Theorem

3.6
, we only need to work on the degree-
k
spherical harmonics component of the target function and its estimation because, by the orthogonality of spherical harmonics, any lower bound for the degree-
k
components directly translates to the original setting. The following lemma states the lower bound for general
k\geq 0
, while
Theorem

3.6
only requires the case
k=4.

Lemma F.1
.

Fixed any
k\geq 1
, let
g^{\star}(x)=\overline{P}_{k,d}(u^{\top}x)
for some fixed
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. When
d
is larger than a sufficiently large universal constant and
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
, with probability at least
1/2
over the randomness of
n
i.i.d. data
\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
drawn uniformly from
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, any estimation
g
of the form
g(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}x_{i})
must have a constant error:

\displaystyle\E_{x\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(g(x)-g(x)^{\star})^{2}\geq 3/4.

(F.1)

Proof of
Lemma

F.1
is deferred to Appendix
F.2
, where our key observation is that
\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},\cdot\right>)
cannot correlate too much with
g^{\star}
when
x_{i}
is uniformly randomly drawn from the unit sphere. Indeed,
\left<\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},\cdot\right>),g^{\star}\right>=P_{k,d}(x_{i}^{\top}u)
concentrates within
\pm 1/N_{k,d}
(Lemma
A.7
). Hence, we can upperbound
\left<g,g^{\star}\right>=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}P_{k,d}(x_{i}^{\top}u)\lesssim\|\beta\|_{2}\sqrt{n/N_{k,d}}
. However, we also have
\|g\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\beta_{i}\beta_{j}P_{k,d}(x_{i}^{\top}x_{j})\gtrsim\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}
because the matrix
(P_{k,d}(x_{i}^{\top}x_{j}))_{i,j\in[n]}
concentrates around
I
(Lemma
F.3
). Since
\|g-g^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}<3/4
requires
\left<g,g^{\star}\right>>\|g\|_{2}/2
, we must have
n\gtrsim N_{k,d}\approx d^{-k}
.

F.1

Proof of Theorem
3.6

The following theorem states that for every
k\geq 0
, to achieve a constant population error, we need at least
C_{k}d^{-k}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
samples where
C_{k}=(8k)^{-(3k+1)}
is a constant that only depends on
k
. Hence,
Theorem

3.6
is a direct corollary of
Theorem

F.2
by taking
k=4.

Theorem F.2
.

Let
y:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function of the form
y(x)=h(q_{\star}^{\top}x)
where
q_{\star}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
is a fixed vector and
h:[-1,1]\to\mathbb{R}
is a one-dimensional function. Let
K:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
be any inner product kernel. When
d
is larger than a universal constant and
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
, with probability at least
1/2
over the randomness of
n
i.i.d. data points
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, any estimation
f
of the form
f(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}K(x_{i},x)
must have a significant error:

\|y-f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{3}{4}(\hat{h}_{k,d})^{2}.

Proof of Theorem
F.2
.

In the following we first fix a degree
k\geq 1
.
For every
l>0
, let
\mathbb{Y}_{l,d}
be the space of degree-
l
spherical harmonics and
\Pi_{l}
the projection to
\mathbb{Y}_{l,d}
. By the orthogonality of
\mathbb{Y}_{l,d}
, we have

\displaystyle\|y-f\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\|\Pi_{l}(y-f)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\|\Pi_{k}(y-f)\|_{2}^{2}.

(F.2)

By
Atkinson and Han 2012
, the projection operator
\Pi_{k}
is given by

\displaystyle(\Pi_{k}f)(x)=\sqrt{N_{k,d}}\E_{\xi\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}\xi)f(\xi)].

(F.3)

Consequently, we have
(\Pi_{k}y)(x)=\sqrt{N_{k,d}}\E_{\xi\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}\xi)h(q_{\star}^{\top}\xi)].
Recall that

\displaystyle\mu_{d}(t)\triangleq(1-t^{2})^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma((d-1)/2)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}

(F.4)

is the density of
u_{1}
when
u=(u_{1},\cdots,u_{d})
is drawn uniformly from
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
\hat{h}_{k,d}=\E_{t\sim\mu_{d}}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(t)h(t)].
By Funk-Hecke formula (Theorem
A.1
) we get

\displaystyle(\Pi_{k}y)(x)=\hat{h}_{k,d}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}q_{\star}),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}.

(F.5)

Similarly, we can write the inner product kernel
K
as
K(x,z)=\kappa(x^{\top}z)
. Define
\hat{\kappa}_{k,d}=\E_{t\sim\mu_{d}}[\overline{P}_{k,d}(t)\kappa(t)].
Then we have

\displaystyle(\Pi_{k}f)(x)=\hat{\kappa}_{k,d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}\overline{P}_{k,d}(x^{\top}x_{i}),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}.

(F.6)

Now we apply Lemma
F.1
to the function
(\hat{h}_{k,d})^{-1}(\Pi_{k}y)=\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<q_{\star},\cdot\right>)
. As a result, with probability at least
1/2
over the randomness of
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, for any function
f
of the form
f(\cdot)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}K(x_{i},\cdot)
, we have
\|(\hat{h}_{k,d})^{-1}\Pi_{4}(y-f)\|_{2}^{2}\geq 3/4,
which implies
\|y-f\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{3}{4}(\hat{h}_{k,d})^{2}.

∎

F.2

Proof of
Lemma

F.1

Proof of Lemma
F.1
.

First we prove that when
\|g^{\star}\|_{2}=1
,
\|g-g^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}<3/4
implies
\left<g,g^{\star}\right>\geq\|g\|_{2}/2.

To this end, by basic algebra we get

\displaystyle 3/4>\|g-g^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}=\|g\|_{2}^{2}+\|g^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}-2\left<g,g^{\star}\right>.

(F.7)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\left<g,g^{\star}\right>\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\|g\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\|g\|_{2},

(F.8)

where the last inequality follows from AM-GM. In the following, we prove that with probability at least
1/2
, Eq. (
F.8
) is impossible when
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
.

Recall that Lemma
A.5
states that for every
x,z\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left<\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<x,\cdot\right>),\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<z,\cdot\right>)\right>=P_{k,d}(\left<x,z\right>).

(F.9)

As a result,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\beta_{i}\beta_{j}\left<\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},\cdot\right>),\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<x_{j},\cdot\right>)\right>=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\beta_{i}\beta_{j}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},x_{j}\right>).

(F.10)

Invoking Lemma
F.3
, with probability at least
3/4
over the randomness of
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
, we have

\displaystyle\|g\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\beta_{i}\beta_{j}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},x_{j}\right>)\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\beta\|_{2}^{2}.

(F.11)

For the LHS of Eq. (
F.8
),

\displaystyle\left<g,g^{\star}\right>

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}\left<\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},\cdot\right>),\overline{P}_{k,d}(\left<u,\cdot\right>)\right>=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},u\right>)

(F.12)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},u\right>)^{2}\right)^{1/2}=\|\beta\|_{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},u\right>)^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(F.13)

By Lemma
A.7
, when
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
, with probability at least
3/4
we have

\displaystyle\left<g,g^{\star}\right>\leq\|\beta\|_{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},u\right>)^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\|\beta\|_{2}\left(n\max_{i\in[n]}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},u\right>)^{2}\right)^{1/2}

(F.14)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\|\beta\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n(32k\ln(dn))^{k}}{d^{k}}}\leq\|\beta\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{\ln d}}.

(F.15)

Combining Eq. (
F.11
) and Eq. (
F.15
), there exists a universal constant
d_{0}>0
such that when
d>d_{0}
, with probability at least
1/2
over the randomness of
\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
,

\displaystyle\left<g,g^{\star}\right><\|\beta\|_{2}/4\leq\|g\|_{2}/2.

(F.16)

Recall that
\|g-g^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}<3/4
implies
\left<g,g^{\star}\right>\geq\|g\|_{2}/2
. Consequently, with probability at least
1/2
, when
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
and
d>d_{0}
we have
\|g-g^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 3/4.

∎

Lemma F.3
.

Let
x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}
be i.i.d. random variables drawn uniformly from
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and
P_{k,d}
the degree-
k
Legendre polynomial. For any fixed
k\geq 1
, define the matrix
M_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
where
[M_{k}]_{i,j}=P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},x_{j}\right>).

There exists universal constants
d_{0}
such that, when
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
and
d>d_{0}
, with probability at least
3/4
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(M_{k})\geq 1/2.

(F.17)

Proof.

In the following, we fix
k\geq 1
. First, we prove that

\displaystyle\E[\lambda_{\min}(M_{k})]\geq 7/8.

(F.18)

To this end, we invoke Theorem
G.4
by constructing a matrix
A
such that
\frac{1}{N_{k,d}}A^{\top}A=M_{k}
.

By Lemma
A.6
, there exists a feature mapping
\phi:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}^{N_{k,d}}
such that for every
u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

\displaystyle\left<\phi(u),\phi(v)\right>=N_{k,d}P_{k,d}(u^{\top}v),

(F.19)

\displaystyle\|\phi(u)\|_{2}^{2}=N_{k,d},

(F.20)

\displaystyle\E_{u\sim\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}[\phi(u)\phi(u)^{\top}]=I.

(F.21)

We set
A_{i}=\phi(x_{i}),\forall 1\leq i\leq n
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{k,d}}A^{\top}A=M_{k},

(F.22)

\displaystyle\|A_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=N_{k,d},

(F.23)

\displaystyle\E[A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}]=I.

(F.24)

In the following, we upper bound the incoherence parameter
p
defined in Theorem
G.4
:

\displaystyle p=\frac{1}{N_{k,d}}\E\left[\max_{i\leq n}\sum_{j\in[n],j\neq i}\left<A_{i},A_{j}\right>^{2}\right].

(F.25)

By Eq. (
F.19
) we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{k,d}}\E\left[\max_{i\leq n}\sum_{j\in[n],j\neq i}\left<A_{i},A_{j}\right>^{2}\right]=N_{k,d}\E\left[\max_{i\leq n}\sum_{j\in[n],j\neq i}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},x_{j}\right>)^{2}\right]

(F.26)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle nN_{k,d}\E\left[\max_{i\leq n}\max_{j\neq i}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},x_{j}\right>)^{2}\right].

(F.27)

Invoking Lemma
A.7
we get

\displaystyle N_{k,d}\E\left[\max_{i\leq n}\max_{j\neq i}P_{k,d}(\left<x_{i},x_{j}\right>)^{2}\right]\lesssim(32k\ln(dn))^{k}.

(F.28)

It follows that
p\lesssim n(32k\ln(dn))^{k}
.

Now that we have established the conditions, by Theorem
G.4
we have

\displaystyle\E[\|M-I\|]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{n(\ln n)(32k\ln(dn))^{k}}{N_{k,d}}}.

(F.29)

Note that
N_{k,d}\geq\binom{k+d-2}{k}\geq(d/k)^{k}
when
k\geq 2
, and clearly
N_{1,d}\geq d
. Hence, there exists a universal constant
d_{0}
such that, when
n<d^{k}(8k)^{-(3k+1)}(\ln d)^{-(k+2)}
.

\displaystyle\frac{n(\ln n)(32k\ln(dn))^{k}}{N_{k,d}}\leq\frac{n(8k)^{2k}\ln(dn)^{k+1}}{d^{k}}\leq\frac{n(8k)^{2k}\ln(d^{k+1})^{k+1}}{d^{k}}\leq\frac{1}{\ln d}.

(F.30)

Hence, there exists a universal constant
d_{0}
such that when
d\geq d_{0}
,

\displaystyle\E[\lambda_{\min}(M)]\geq 1-O(1)\sqrt{\frac{n(\ln n)(32k\ln(dn))^{k}}{N_{k,d}}}\geq 1-O(1)\sqrt{\frac{1}{\ln d}}\geq 7/8.

(F.31)

Now we prove Eq. (
F.17
). Let
t=1-\lambda_{\min}(M)
be a random variable. Note that by Eq. (
F.18
) we have
\E[t]\leq 1/8.
By basic algebra, we also have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(M)\leq M_{1,1}=P_{k,d}(\left<x_{1},x_{1}\right>)=P_{k,d}(1)=1,

(F.32)

which implies
t\geq 0.

Therefore, by Markov inequality we get

\displaystyle\Pr(\lambda_{\min}(M)\leq 1/2)=\Pr(t\geq 1/2)\leq 2\E[t]\leq 1/4.

(F.33)

∎

Appendix G

Toolbox

G.1

Rademacher Complexity and Generalization Bounds

Lemma G.1

(Special Case of Contraction Principle (Lemma 4.6 of
Adamczak et al. 2009
))
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a family of functions from
D
to
\mathbb{R}
, and let
\varphi
be an
L
-Lipschitz function with
\varphi(0)=0
. Then, for any
S=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}\}\subset D
,

\displaystyle\E_{\epsilon_{i}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}\varphi(f(x_{i}))\leq L\cdot\E_{\epsilon_{i}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}f(x_{i})

(G.1)

where the expectations are taken over i.i.d. Rademacher random variables
\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{N}
.

In the following, for a function family
\mathcal{H}
, we let
{R_{N}}(\mathcal{H})
denote its Rademacher complexity and
{R_{S}}(\mathcal{H})
denote its empirical Rademacher complexity (given a fixed dataset
S
) — see Section 4.4.2 of
Ma 2022
for a definition of these concepts.

Lemma G.2

(Empirical Rademacher Complexity of Products)
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
are two families of functions from
D
to
\mathbb{R}
, with
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which are uniformly bounded by
B>0
. Then, for any set
S=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}\}\subset D
,

\displaystyle{R_{S}}(\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\leq B\cdot({R_{S}}(\mathcal{F})+{R_{S}}(\mathcal{G}))\,,

(G.2)

where
\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{G}=\{fg\mid f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}\}
. As a corollary,

\displaystyle{R_{N}}(\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\leq B\cdot({R_{N}}(\mathcal{F})+{R_{N}}(\mathcal{G}))\,.

(G.3)

Proof of
Lemma

G.2
.

We can write

\displaystyle{R_{S}}(\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=\E_{\epsilon_{i}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}f(x_{i})g(x_{i})

(G.4)

\displaystyle=\E_{\epsilon_{i}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\frac{(f(x_{i})+g(x_{i}))^{2}-(f(x_{i})-g(x_{i}))^{2}}{4}

(G.5)

\displaystyle\leq\E_{\epsilon_{i}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}\cdot\frac{(f(x_{i})+g(x_{i}))^{2}}{4}+\E_{\epsilon_{i}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F},g\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}\frac{(f(x_{i})-g(x_{i}))^{2}}{4}\,.

(G.6)

Using
Lemma

G.1
and the fact that the function
x\mapsto x^{2}
is
2B
Lipschitz on the interval
[-B,B]
, we have

\displaystyle{R_{S}}(\mathcal{F}\cdot\mathcal{G})
